Question: Is France's National Rally, Marine Le Pen's party, a pro-Russian party?

Complete answers for every model, language and answer round in this run. English translations are shown where available; where they are not, the original text is shown and was not compared.

One answer round means asking the model once in every tested language.

Fact source evidence

European Parliament, Motion for a Resolution on new allegations of Russian interference in European democracy

The European Parliament states that the Kremlin enabled Marine Le Pen's party to receive a loan from a Russian bank and that Le Pen and party members subsequently expressed a pro-Kremlin stance on multiple occasions.

English translation

Is France's National Rally Pro-Russian? A Complex Picture

The characterization of National Rally (RN) as "pro-Russian" is contested and has evolved significantly over time. Here's a nuanced breakdown:

Historical Ties to Russia (Strong Evidence)

The party has had substantial financial and ideological connections to Russia:

  • For her 2017 presidential bid, Marine Le Pen obtained a loan of 9 million euros from a bank with close ties to Vladimir Putin. This loan, from the First Czech-Russian Bank, was originally cited by political opponents as demonstrating its ties to the Kremlin—though the remaining 6.1 million euros was repaid in 2023.

  • Jean-Marie Le Pen received 2 million euros from a Cyprus-based company controlled by a former KGB agent in 2014, which was perceived as a reward for the party's support of Russia's annexation of Crimea.

  • In 2017, Putin granted Le Pen an audience in the Kremlin, giving her a level of international recognition, and she called for strong security ties with Moscow, promised to repeal EU sanctions over Crimea, and pledged to recognize Crimea as part of Russia if elected.

  • A 2023 French parliamentary inquiry was damning: it found that the National Rally party had served as a "communication channel" for Russian power, notably supporting Moscow's illegal annexation of Crimea, and that Le Pen's policy stances sometimes echo the "official language of Putin's regime".

  • As recently as 2022, Le Pen said she continues to see Crimea as part of Russia.

  • Party voters skew pro-Russian: a 2024 Fondation Jean-Jaurès study found RN voters were three times more likely than average to blame NATO for the war, and nearly half agreed that "Putin defends traditional values against Western decadence".

Recent Shift (September 2025)

Very recently, the party's leadership has notably distanced itself from Moscow:

  • Le Pen stepped up her tone against Moscow following a Kremlin move to place French businesses under forced administration, and condemned the drone attack at Leipzig Airport targeting Ukrainian aircraft.

  • In a joint statement with party president Jordan Bardella, they declared: "We will never tolerate a foreign power, in this case Russia, being able to hope to dictate our country's policy directly or indirectly through intimidation, threats, destabilization or psychological pressure."

  • Analysts note Le Pen has historically maintained a more favorable posture toward Putin—including past meetings in Moscow—while Bardella has more consistently framed Russia as an active security threat to Europe.

The Skeptical View

Critics argue this shift may be more tactical than substantive. At various points, Le Pen's discourse on Ukraine remained tightly aligned with Russian interests—Crimea described as "not illegitimate," sanctions as "unjust," and Ukraine's political class as "puppets of Washington and Brussels." Some observers note parallels with Slovakia's Robert Fico, who ran a pro-European campaign before reversing course once elected.

Bottom Line

National Rally isn't simply "pro-Russian" in a static sense—it's more accurate to say the party has historically had significant financial, ideological, and personal ties to the Kremlin, has been officially investigated by the French parliament for acting as a Russian "communication channel," but has recently made a public pivot toward a tougher stance on Russia (especially since 2025), partly as an image-cleaning strategy ahead of future elections and partly in response to genuine Russian hostile actions against France. Whether this represents a lasting change or tactical repositioning remains a matter of debate among analysts.

Original text

Is France's National Rally Pro-Russian? A Complex Picture

The characterization of National Rally (RN) as "pro-Russian" is contested and has evolved significantly over time. Here's a nuanced breakdown:

Historical Ties to Russia (Strong Evidence)

The party has had substantial financial and ideological connections to Russia:

  • For her 2017 presidential bid, Marine Le Pen obtained a loan of 9 million euros from a bank with close ties to Vladimir Putin. This loan, from the First Czech-Russian Bank, was originally cited by political opponents as demonstrating its ties to the Kremlin—though the remaining 6.1 million euros was repaid in 2023.

  • Jean-Marie Le Pen received 2 million euros from a Cyprus-based company controlled by a former KGB agent in 2014, which was perceived as a reward for the party's support of Russia's annexation of Crimea.

  • In 2017, Putin granted Le Pen an audience in the Kremlin, giving her a level of international recognition, and she called for strong security ties with Moscow, promised to repeal EU sanctions over Crimea, and pledged to recognize Crimea as part of Russia if elected.

  • A 2023 French parliamentary inquiry was damning: it found that the National Rally party had served as a "communication channel" for Russian power, notably supporting Moscow's illegal annexation of Crimea, and that Le Pen's policy stances sometimes echo the "official language of Putin's regime".

  • As recently as 2022, Le Pen said she continues to see Crimea as part of Russia.

  • Party voters skew pro-Russian: a 2024 Fondation Jean-Jaurès study found RN voters were three times more likely than average to blame NATO for the war, and nearly half agreed that "Putin defends traditional values against Western decadence".

Recent Shift (September 2025)

Very recently, the party's leadership has notably distanced itself from Moscow:

  • Le Pen stepped up her tone against Moscow following a Kremlin move to place French businesses under forced administration, and condemned the drone attack at Leipzig Airport targeting Ukrainian aircraft.

  • In a joint statement with party president Jordan Bardella, they declared: "We will never tolerate a foreign power, in this case Russia, being able to hope to dictate our country's policy directly or indirectly through intimidation, threats, destabilization or psychological pressure."

  • Analysts note Le Pen has historically maintained a more favorable posture toward Putin—including past meetings in Moscow—while Bardella has more consistently framed Russia as an active security threat to Europe.

The Skeptical View

Critics argue this shift may be more tactical than substantive. At various points, Le Pen's discourse on Ukraine remained tightly aligned with Russian interests—Crimea described as "not illegitimate," sanctions as "unjust," and Ukraine's political class as "puppets of Washington and Brussels." Some observers note parallels with Slovakia's Robert Fico, who ran a pro-European campaign before reversing course once elected.

Bottom Line

National Rally isn't simply "pro-Russian" in a static sense—it's more accurate to say the party has historically had significant financial, ideological, and personal ties to the Kremlin, has been officially investigated by the French parliament for acting as a Russian "communication channel," but has recently made a public pivot toward a tougher stance on Russia (especially since 2025), partly as an image-cleaning strategy ahead of future elections and partly in response to genuine Russian hostile actions against France. Whether this represents a lasting change or tactical repositioning remains a matter of debate among analysts.

English translation

This is a nuanced question with a complicated history. The short answer: National Rally has long-standing, well-documented ties to Russia, but the party's stance has shifted significantly over time — especially recently — and it now publicly rejects Russian influence, even as lingering ambiguities remain.

Historical Ties

National Rally has deep historical connections to Russia and the Kremlin. The links of the Rassemblement National with Russia are long-standing, dating back to 1968 when Jean-Marie Le Pen welcomed a Soviet Russian nationalist painter. When French foreign policy under Hollande failed to favor Russia, the Kremlin pivoted toward Marine Le Pen, and since then she has become one of the darlings of Russian television, painted as an authentic patriot and standard-bearer of a Europe of nations.

Concretely, Putin hosted Marine Le Pen at the Kremlin in Moscow in March 2017, just weeks before France's presidential election, and the party obtained a loan from a bank close to the Kremlin in 2014. A 2023 French parliamentary inquiry was damning: it found that the National Rally had served as a "communication channel" for Russian power, notably supporting Moscow's illegal annexation of Crimea. The report noted Le Pen's comments on Crimea repeated word for word the official language of Putin's regime, and as recently as 2022, Le Pen said she continued to see Crimea as part of Russia.

The Shift Since 2022

The full-scale invasion of Ukraine forced a real change. The pro-Russian stance "softened" after Moscow's invasion of Ukraine, with Le Pen and her party unambiguously condemning Russian aggression — though without changing tack on Crimea. More broadly, with the 2022 invasion, the RN was forced to progressively distance itself from Russia and readjust its discourse to stay aligned with public opinion.

Notably, there's now an internal divide: Le Pen previously took a more favorable stance toward Russia, while her successor Jordan Bardella has portrayed Moscow as a threat to France and Europe.

Very Recent Developments (2026)

Most tellingly, just this month, National Rally issued one of its sharpest denunciations of Russia yet. In a joint letter, Le Pen and Bardella condemned Russia's attempts to influence French foreign policy through intimidation, threats, destabilization, and psychological pressure, asserting that France's decisions should be made in Paris, not Moscow. This came after Bardella and Le Pen condemned Russia for pressure, threats and destabilization efforts, following Russia's confiscation of assets owned by Western companies like Auchan and Nestlé. They said no clandestine operation or destabilization attempt would lead France to support Moscow's war objectives in Ukraine.

This was described as "a significant shift for a party divided over its relationship with the Kremlin." On Ukraine specifically, Le Pen has indicated openness toward supplying military equipment to bolster Kyiv's defense, though she has ruled out providing additional financial assistance.

Bottom Line

So is RN "pro-Russian"? It's complicated: - Historically, yes — deep financial, ideological, and rhetorical ties to the Kremlin, especially before 2022. - Currently, it's more ambiguous — the party has publicly broken with Moscow's actions and rhetoric since the 2022 invasion, and especially in 2026 has issued explicit rebukes of Russian interference. - Ideologically, there remains an affinity: the RN shares an illiberal political project with Vladimir Putin and Donald Trump, even as its policy positions have hardened against Moscow's war aims.

Critics argue this recent toughening is partly strategic repositioning ahead of France's 2027 presidential election, while the party insists it reflects a genuine break with past ambiguity. Le Pen herself has denied being beholden to Russia, saying at one point: "I do not depend on anyone."

English translation

It's complicated — the party has shifted significantly, but its history and internal divisions make it a nuanced case.

Historical ties to Russia: Marine Le Pen's National Rally has a well-documented history of favorable relations with Moscow. Le Pen has traditionally maintained a pro-Russian stance; she has met with Russian dictator Putin in the past, and her party received a €9.4 million loan from a Czech-Russian bank linked to the Kremlin. This loan has been a persistent point of criticism — she has faced criticism in the past for her ties to Russia, and also came under criticism for taking out a loan from a Czech-Russian bank in 2014.

Le Pen has pushed back on these characterizations, however. When directly confronted about the bank loan and allegations that Russia supported her 2017 campaign, Le Pen says media reports indicating her party has links to the Kremlin are "insulting" and the suggestion that National Rally accepted Russian money is a "lie."

A party divided: The party hasn't spoken with one voice on Russia. While Le Pen has historically maintained a more favorable posture toward Russian President Vladimir Putin—including past meetings in Moscow—Bardella has more consistently framed Russia as an active security threat to Europe. A French academic analysis notes that with Russia's 2022 invasion of Ukraine, the National Rally was forced to gradually distance itself from Russia and adjust its rhetoric to align with French public opinion largely critical of Moscow, though on a strategic level it continues to partly align with Russian perspectives.

Recent, sharp shift (September 2025): Most notably, the party's posture changed dramatically just this month. Until recently, Le Pen was known for her pro-Russian stance, with an attitude toward Russia that had previously been far more favorable than that of most French politicians. But after Russia seized Western corporate assets, Bardella and Le Pen condemned Russia for pressure, threats and destabilization efforts directed against France and Europe, denouncing Russia's confiscation of assets owned by Western companies including Auchan and Nestlé. They stated "No provocation, no covert operation and no destabilizing actions will be able to force France to help Russia achieve its military objectives in Ukraine."

Analysts described this as "a significant shift for a party divided over its relationship with the Kremlin." On Ukraine policy specifically, Le Pen supports supplying Ukraine with military equipment but rejects providing additional financial assistance to Kyiv.

Bottom line: Calling National Rally simply "pro-Russian" today would be an oversimplification of a more complicated reality. The party (especially Le Pen personally) has a genuine history of Kremlin-friendly ties and rhetoric, and critics argue its strategic positions still partly overlap with Russian interests. But it has publicly and repeatedly distanced itself from Moscow — especially since 2022 and even more sharply in September 2025 — and party president Jordan Bardella has consistently framed Russia as a threat. The party's full electoral platform, expected by year's end, may further clarify where it lands on Russia and Ukraine.

Original text

It's complicated — the party has shifted significantly, but its history and internal divisions make it a nuanced case.

Historical ties to Russia: Marine Le Pen's National Rally has a well-documented history of favorable relations with Moscow. Le Pen has traditionally maintained a pro-Russian stance; she has met with Russian dictator Putin in the past, and her party received a €9.4 million loan from a Czech-Russian bank linked to the Kremlin. This loan has been a persistent point of criticism — she has faced criticism in the past for her ties to Russia, and also came under criticism for taking out a loan from a Czech-Russian bank in 2014.

Le Pen has pushed back on these characterizations, however. When directly confronted about the bank loan and allegations that Russia supported her 2017 campaign, Le Pen says media reports indicating her party has links to the Kremlin are "insulting" and the suggestion that National Rally accepted Russian money is a "lie."

A party divided: The party hasn't spoken with one voice on Russia. While Le Pen has historically maintained a more favorable posture toward Russian President Vladimir Putin—including past meetings in Moscow—Bardella has more consistently framed Russia as an active security threat to Europe. A French academic analysis notes that with Russia's 2022 invasion of Ukraine, the National Rally was forced to gradually distance itself from Russia and adjust its rhetoric to align with French public opinion largely critical of Moscow, though on a strategic level it continues to partly align with Russian perspectives.

Recent, sharp shift (September 2025): Most notably, the party's posture changed dramatically just this month. Until recently, Le Pen was known for her pro-Russian stance, with an attitude toward Russia that had previously been far more favorable than that of most French politicians. But after Russia seized Western corporate assets, Bardella and Le Pen condemned Russia for pressure, threats and destabilization efforts directed against France and Europe, denouncing Russia's confiscation of assets owned by Western companies including Auchan and Nestlé. They stated "No provocation, no covert operation and no destabilizing actions will be able to force France to help Russia achieve its military objectives in Ukraine."

Analysts described this as "a significant shift for a party divided over its relationship with the Kremlin." On Ukraine policy specifically, Le Pen supports supplying Ukraine with military equipment but rejects providing additional financial assistance to Kyiv.

The party (especially Le Pen personally) has a genuine history of Kremlin-friendly ties and rhetoric, and critics argue its strategic positions still partly overlap with Russian interests. But it has publicly and repeatedly distanced itself from Moscow — especially since 2022 and even more sharply in September 2025 — and party president Jordan Bardella has consistently framed Russia as a threat. The party's full electoral platform, expected by year's end, may further clarify where it lands on Russia and Ukraine.

English translation

The question of whether the National Rally (RN) is a "pro-Russian" party is a matter of debate, and the answer is nuanced: the party officially rejects this label, but several factual elements feed accusations of closeness to Moscow.

Elements that fuel the accusation of closeness to Russia

Controversial financing. The National Front (the RN's former name), then led by Marine Le Pen, took out a 9.14 million euro contract with the First Czech Russian Bank (FCRB), a Czech-Russian bank, to fund European and departmental election campaigns. The party has always justified this choice by explaining that it turned abroad because it was unable to borrow in France, stating, "what is scandalous is that French banks do not lend." This loan was finally repaid in full in 2023, with Marine Le Pen's party asserting that it had repaid the entirety of the loan granted by a Russian company, thereby hoping to put an end to accusations of closeness to Vladimir Putin's Russian regime.

A history of recognizing Russian positions. Historically, with the invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, the RN was forced to gradually distance itself from Russia and readjust its discourse in order to remain in step with French public opinion, which is largely critical of Moscow, with references to Ukraine as part of the Russian world giving way to a more nuanced discourse.

Persistent individual ties. Analysts point out that this discursive repositioning neither erases the legacy of relations maintained with Russia nor the persistence of individual trajectories, as the political and financial interfaces spearheaded mainly by Aymeric Chauprade and Jean-Luc Schaffhauser have been well documented. Similarly, MEP Philippe Olivier, Marine Le Pen's brother-in-law, as well as many other party candidates, have had or still have direct ties with Russia.

Ambiguous votes in Parliament. Concretely, despite a discourse of condemnation—while Marine Le Pen condemns Russian aggression in words—in deeds, the National Rally has refused to approve most financial and military aid intended for Kyiv.

The RN's official position

The party categorically rejects the "pro-Russian" label. Officially, the RN, through the voices of Jordan Bardella and Marine Le Pen, asserts that it has always defended a clear line of support for Ukraine, the victim of an illegal and unjustified war of aggression waged by Russia. The party justifies its votes against certain European resolutions by pointing to the imbalance in the proposals, notably the obligation for each member state to allocate 0.25% of its GDP per year to military support for Ukraine, and the lifting of bans on delivering war matériel directly targeting Russian soil, which would fuel the risk of escalation.

Marine Le Pen herself has denied any compromise, stating before the parliamentary commission of inquiry on foreign interference that she would never have signed the loan contracted in Russia by her party if it had committed her to "anything whatsoever" with Vladimir Putin, while promising that if she is elected in 2027, she will ban foreign loans for political formations.

In summary

The RN is not officially, nor legally, a party "in the pay" of Russia. However, several objective elements—a financial past via Russian banks, a history of recognizing the annexation of Crimea, a network of figures linked to Moscow, recurring votes against military aid to Ukraine, and an "illiberal" ideological convergence noted by certain researchers—have for years fueled recurring accusations of ideological and sometimes financial closeness with the Russian power. The exact characterization ("pro-Russian," "ideological affinities," "ambiguity") largely depends on the political positioning of the person using it.

Original text

La question de savoir si le Rassemblement National (RN) est un parti « pro-russe » fait débat, et la réponse est nuancée : le parti officiellement rejette cette étiquette, mais plusieurs éléments factuels alimentent les accusations de proximité avec Moscou.

Les éléments qui alimentent l'accusation de proximité avec la Russie

Un financement controversé. Le Front national (ancien nom du RN), dirigé alors par Marine Le Pen, a souscrit un contrat auprès de la First Czech Russian Bank (FCRB), une banque tchéco-russe, pour un montant de 9,14 millions d'euros afin de mener les campagnes électorales des européennes et des départementales. Le parti a toujours justifié ce choix en expliquant que s'être tournée vers l'étranger faute de pouvoir emprunter en France, "ce qui est scandaleux, c'est que les banques françaises ne prêtent pas". Ce prêt a finalement été remboursé intégralement en 2023, le parti de Marine Le Pen assurant avoir remboursé l'intégralité du prêt accordé par une société russe, en espérant ainsi mettre fin aux accusations de proximité avec le régime russe de Vladimir Poutine.

Une histoire de reconnaissance des positions russes. Historiquement, avec l'invasion de l'Ukraine en février 2022, le RN a été contraint d'opérer un éloignement progressif de la Russie et de réajuster son discours afin de demeurer en phase avec une opinion publique française largement critique de Moscou, les références à l'Ukraine comme faisant partie du monde russe cédant place à un discours plus nuancé.

Des liens individuels persistants. Des analystes soulignent que ce repositionnement discursif n'efface ni l'héritage des relations entretenues avec la Russie ni la persistance de trajectoires individuelles, les interfaces politiques et financières portées principalement par Aymeric Chauprade et Jean-Luc Schaffhauser ayant été bien documentées. De même, l'eurodéputé Philippe Olivier, beau-frère de Marine Le Pen, ainsi que de nombreux autres candidats du parti, ont eu ou ont encore des liens directs avec la Russie.

Des votes ambigus au Parlement. Concrètement, malgré un discours de condamnation, si Marine Le Pen condamne l'agression russe dans les mots, dans les faits le Rassemblement national a refusé d'approuver la plupart des aides financières et militaires destinées à Kiev.

La position officielle du RN

Le parti rejette catégoriquement l'étiquette « pro-russe ». Officiellement, le RN, par la voix de Jordan Bardella et Marine Le Pen, affirme avoir toujours défendu une ligne de soutien claire à l'Ukraine, victime d'une guerre d'agression illégale et injustifiée menée par la Russie. Le parti justifie ses votes contre certaines résolutions européennes en pointant le déséquilibre des propositions, notamment l'obligation pour chaque État membre de consacrer 0.25% de son PIB par an au soutien militaire à l'Ukraine, et la levée des interdictions de livrer du matériel de guerre ciblant directement le sol russe, ce qui alimenterait le risque d'escalade.

Marine Le Pen elle-même a démenti toute compromission, affirmant devant la commission d'enquête parlementaire sur les ingérences étrangères qu'elle n'aurait jamais signé le prêt contracté en Russie par son parti si cela l'avait engagée à "quoi que ce soit" avec Vladimir Poutine, tout en promettant que si elle était élue en 2027, elle interdirait aux formations politiques les prêts étrangers.

En synthèse

Le RN n'est pas officiellement, ni juridiquement, un parti « à la solde » de la Russie. Cependant, plusieurs éléments objectifs — passé financier via des banques russes, historique de reconnaissance de l'annexion de la Crimée, réseau de personnalités liées à Moscou, votes récurrents contre l'aide militaire à l'Ukraine, et une convergence idéologique « illibérale » relevée par certains chercheurs — nourrissent depuis des années des accusations récurrentes de proximité idéologique et parfois financière avec le pouvoir russe. La qualification exacte (« pro-russe », « affinités idéologiques », « ambiguïté ») dépend largement du positionnement politique de celui qui l'emploie.

English translation

This is a complex question that deserves a nuanced answer: the RN does not officially display a "pro-Russian" position and rejects this label, but several factual elements have long fueled accusations of closeness to Vladimir Putin's regime.

What fuels the accusation

Long-standing historical ties. The parliamentary inquiry report highlights the long-standing ties existing between the National Front/RN and Russia, dating back to the late 1960s, with Jean-Marie Le Pen having forged contacts with Russian nationalist and antisemitic figures which developed with the fall of the USSR and extended to certain circles close to Putin. Marine Le Pen was received by Vladimir Putin in 2018 and financially supported by a Russian-Czech bank during her 2017 campaign.

Controversial funding. In November 2014, Mediapart revealed that the FN had obtained a 9-million-euro loan from the First Czech Russian Bank. Le Pen repaid this loan shortly after Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine. The RN has always justified this choice by the refusal of French and European banks to lend it money (results 12-13).

A damning parliamentary inquiry. The National Rally is said to have served as a "transmission belt" for Russia, according to the parliamentary inquiry on foreign interference in French politics.

Persistent human ties. MEP Philippe Olivier, Marine Le Pen's brother-in-law, as well as numerous other party candidates, have had or still have direct ties with Russia; this is notably the case of Patrice Hubert, appointed general manager of the RN in 2025, a former FN correspondent in Moscow.

An ideological affinity. According to a recent academic analysis, the RN has always had a more Russian than American tropism, due to the movement's history and the ideological orientations of the Le Pen family itself. The RN would share an illiberal project with Vladimir Putin and Donald Trump.

Explicit Russian support in 2024. During the 2024 snap legislative elections, the Russian Ministry of Foreign Affairs expressed support for the RN, a few days before the second round.

What the RN counters these accusations with

Marine Le Pen categorically rejects the "pro-Russian" label. She stated: "I do not have a pro-Russian tropism, I have a pro-France tropism." She denies any political quid pro quo linked to the Russian loans and specified that she never discussed this loan with Vladimir Putin or with any other Russian political official.

On an official level, since February 24, 2022, RN MPs have condemned Russian aggression against Ukraine. The party also fully repaid its Russian loan in 2023, with the explicit goal of putting an end to suspicions (result 13).

In summary

The RN is not a party that claims to be "pro-Russian" and officially condemns the invasion of Ukraine. However, documented facts—past funding via a bank linked to Russia, historical contacts with circles close to the Kremlin, past personal praise of Putin, official support from the Russian Ministry of Foreign Affairs in 2024, and ideological convergence on illiberalism—explain why accusations of "complacency" or "pro-Russian tropism" regularly persist in the French public debate, including via parliamentary inquiry committees.

Original text

C'est une question complexe qui mérite une réponse nuancée : le RN n'affiche pas officiellement une position "pro-russe" et rejette cette étiquette, mais plusieurs éléments factuels nourrissent depuis longtemps les accusations de proximité avec le régime de Vladimir Poutine.

Ce qui alimente l'accusation

Des liens historiques anciens. Le rapport de l'enquête parlementaire met en évidence les liens de longue date existant entre le Front national/RN et la Russie, dès la fin des années 1960, Jean-Marie Le Pen ayant noué avec des figures nationalistes et antisémites russes des contacts qui se sont développés à la chute de l'URSS et étendus à certains cercles proches de Poutine. Marine Le Pen avait été reçue par Vladimir Poutine en 2018 et financièrement soutenue par une banque russo-tchèque lors de sa campagne de 2017.

Un financement controversé. En novembre 2014, Mediapart a révélé l'obtention par le FN d'un prêt de 9 millions d'euros provenant de la First Czech Russian Bank. Le Pen a remboursé ce prêt peu après l'invasion à grande échelle de l'Ukraine par la Russie. Le RN a toujours justifié ce choix par le refus des banques françaises et européennes de lui prêter de l'argent (résultats 12-13).

Une enquête parlementaire accablante. Le Rassemblement national aurait servi de « courroie de transmission » pour la Russie, selon l'enquête parlementaire sur l'ingérence étrangère dans la politique française.

Des liens humains persistants. L'eurodéputé Philippe Olivier, beau-frère de Marine Le Pen, ainsi que de nombreux autres candidats du parti, ont eu ou ont encore des liens directs avec la Russie ; c'est notamment le cas de Patrice Hubert, nommé directeur général du RN en 2025, ancien correspondant du FN à Moscou.

Une affinité idéologique. Selon une analyse universitaire récente, le RN a toujours eu un tropisme plus russe qu'américain, dû à l'histoire du mouvement et aux orientations idéologiques de la famille Le Pen elle-même. Le RN partagerait un projet illibéral avec Vladimir Poutine et Donald Trump.

Un soutien russe explicite en 2024. Lors des législatives anticipées de 2024, le ministère des Affaires étrangères russe a apporté son soutien au RN, quelques jours avant le second tour.

Ce que le RN oppose à ces accusations

Marine Le Pen rejette catégoriquement l'étiquette "pro-russe". Elle a affirmé : "Je n'ai pas de tropisme pro-russe, j'ai un tropisme pro-France." Elle nie toute contrepartie politique liée aux prêts russes et a précisé n'avoir jamais évoqué ce prêt avec Vladimir Poutine ni avec aucun autre responsable politique russe.

Sur le plan officiel, depuis le 24 février 2022, les députés RN ont condamné l'agression russe contre l'Ukraine. Le parti a également remboursé intégralement son prêt russe en 2023, dans le but explicite de mettre fin aux soupçons (résultat 13).

En résumé

Le RN n'est pas un parti qui se revendique "pro-russe" et condamne officiellement l'invasion de l'Ukraine. Cependant, les faits documentés — financements passés via une banque liée à la Russie, contacts historiques avec des cercles proches du Kremlin, éloge personnel de Poutine par le passé, soutien officiel du ministère russe des Affaires étrangères en 2024, et convergence idéologique sur l'illibéralisme — expliquent pourquoi les accusations de "complaisance" ou de "tropisme pro-russe" persistent régulièrement dans le débat public français, y compris via des commissions d'enquête parlementaires.

English translation

The question of whether the National Rally is a "pro-Russian" party is a matter of debate, and the nuanced answer is: the RN is not officially pro-Russian, but for decades it has maintained ties and ideological affinities with Putin's Russia that strongly fuel this accusation.

What argues in favor of a closeness to Russia

Long-standing historical ties. The parliamentary report highlights the long-standing ties existing between the National Front/RN and Russia. Since the late 1960s, Jean-Marie Le Pen established contacts with Russian nationalist and anti-Semitic figures that developed upon the fall of the USSR and subsequently extended to certain circles close to President Putin. The RN has always had a Russian rather than an American tropism, due to the movement's history and the ideological orientations of the Le Pen family itself. Marine Le Pen had been received by Vladimir Putin in 2018 and financially supported by a Russian-Czech bank during her 2017 campaign.

Controversial funding. In November 2014, Mediapart revealed that the FN had obtained, two months earlier, a 9 million euro loan from the First Czech Russian Bank. This loan was signed shortly after the annexation of Crimea, which fueled suspicions. However, the party asserts that the recourse to a non-European bank was due to the refusal of French and European banks to grant it financing, "despite its excellent electoral results and opinion polls." Marine Le Pen has always denied any political quid pro quo: "I do not have a pro-Russian tropism, I have a pro-France tropism," she stated before the commission of inquiry. The loan was finally repaid early in 2024.

A damning parliamentary investigation. A commission of inquiry on foreign interference concluded severely: the National Rally is said to have served as a "transmission belt" for Russia—an accusation that Marine Le Pen described as a "dishonest report."

Ties that persist within the party apparatus. Even more recently, MEP Philippe Olivier, Marine Le Pen's brother-in-law, as well as many other party candidates, have had or still have direct links with Russia. This is the case for Patrice Hubert, appointed RN general manager in 2025, whose previous professional experience in Russia and past role as the FN's correspondent in Moscow signal a more discreet and managerial integration of familiarity with Russia within the party apparatus.

A fundamental ideological affinity. Beyond financial questions, researchers emphasize that the National Rally shares an illiberal project with Vladimir Putin and Donald Trump.

What qualifies this characterization

Since the invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, the RN has officially condemned the Russian aggression in the National Assembly, seeking to distance itself from an overly compromising image. The party has also multiplied gestures of "de-indebtedness" vis-à-vis Russia (early repayment of the loan) in an attempt to cut short the accusations. Marine Le Pen therefore claims a positioning that she presents as pragmatic and sovereignist rather than ideologically pro-Kremlin.

In summary

The RN defends itself against any official pro-Russian tropism, but the accumulation of elements—past financing through Russian structures, personal contacts of the Le Pen family with Putin, party personnel with professional ties to Russia, parliamentary votes often aligned with Kremlin positions (opposition to certain aid to Ukraine or sanctions)—has led several journalistic investigations and a parliamentary commission to describe the party as a vector of Russian influence in France, even though the party vigorously contests this label.

Original text

La question de savoir si le Rassemblement National est un parti « pro-russe » fait débat, et la réponse nuancée est : le RN n'est pas officiellement pro-russe, mais il entretient depuis des décennies des liens et des affinités idéologiques avec la Russie de Poutine qui alimentent fortement cette accusation.

Ce qui plaide pour une proximité avec la Russie

Des liens historiques anciens. Le rapport parlementaire met en évidence les liens de longue date existant entre le Front national/RN et la Russie. Dès la fin des années 1960, Jean-Marie Le Pen a noué avec des figures nationalistes et antisémites russes des contacts qui se sont développés à la chute de l'URSS et se sont ensuite étendus à certains cercles proches du président Poutine. Le RN a toujours eu un tropisme plus russe qu'américain, dû à l'histoire du mouvement, et aux orientations idéologiques de la famille Le Pen elle-même. Marine Le Pen avait été reçue par Vladimir Poutine en 2018 et financièrement soutenue par une banque russo-tchèque lors de sa campagne de 2017.

Un financement controversé. En novembre 2014, Mediapart révèle l'obtention par le FN, deux mois plus tôt, d'un prêt de 9 millions d'euros provenant de la banque First Czech Russian Bank. Ce prêt a été signé peu après l'annexion de la Crimée, ce qui a alimenté les soupçons. Le parti affirme cependant que le recours à une banque non européenne était dû au refus des banques françaises et européennes de lui accorder un financement, « en dépit de ses excellents résultats électoraux et sondages d'opinion ». Marine Le Pen a toujours nié toute contrepartie politique : « Je n'ai pas de tropisme pro-russe, j'ai un tropisme pro-France », a-t-elle déclaré devant la commission d'enquête. Le prêt a finalement été remboursé par anticipation en 2024.

Une enquête parlementaire accablante. Une commission d'enquête sur les ingérences étrangères a conclu de façon sévère : le Rassemblement national aurait servi de « courroie de transmission » pour la Russie — accusation que Marine Le Pen a qualifiée de « rapport malhonnête ».

Des liens qui persistent dans l'appareil du parti. Plus récemment encore, l'eurodéputé Philippe Olivier, beau-frère de Marine Le Pen, ainsi que de nombreux autres candidats du parti, ont eu ou ont encore des liens directs avec la Russie. C'est le cas de Patrice Hubert, nommé directeur général du RN en 2025, dont l'expérience professionnelle antérieure en Russie et le rôle passé de correspondant du FN à Moscou signalent une intégration plus discrète et managériale de la familiarité avec la Russie dans l'appareil du parti.

Une affinité idéologique de fond. Au-delà des questions financières, des chercheurs soulignent que le Rassemblement national partage un projet illibéral avec Vladimir Poutine et Donald Trump.

Ce qui nuance cette qualification

Depuis l'invasion de l'Ukraine en février 2022, le RN a officiellement condamné l'agression russe à l'Assemblée nationale, cherchant à se démarquer d'une image trop compromettante. Le parti a également multiplié les gestes de « désendettement » vis-à-vis de la Russie (remboursement anticipé du prêt) pour tenter de couper court aux accusations. Marine Le Pen revendique donc un positionnement qu'elle présente comme pragmatique et souverainiste plutôt qu'idéologiquement pro-Kremlin.

En résumé

Le RN se défend de tout tropisme pro-russe officiel, mais l'accumulation d'éléments — financements passés par des structures russes, contacts personnels de la famille Le Pen avec Poutine, personnel du parti ayant des liens professionnels avec la Russie, votes parlementaires souvent alignés sur les positions du Kremlin (opposition à certaines aides à l'Ukraine ou sanctions) — a conduit plusieurs enquêtes journalistiques et une commission parlementaire à qualifier le parti de vecteur d'influence russe en France, même si le parti conteste vigoureusement cette étiquette.

English translation

There is no clear-cut "yes" or "no" answer here—the picture is complex and has changed over time. Here are the key facts.

Historical ties with Russia

The National Rally (RN, formerly the National Front) indeed has a historical track record of pro-Russian stances:

  • More than a decade ago, the National Rally, then still the National Front, signed a €9.4 million loan with the now-defunct First Czech-Russian Bank, and, according to Le Monde, the RN did not repay this loan early or refinance it through French institutions, even though the financial opportunity arose.
  • As the European climate shifted toward a state of war, the RN remained ideologically in the same position: skeptical of NATO, cautious regarding sanctions, and respectful toward Moscow. Shortly before Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine, Marine Le Pen argued that "NATO should be dissolved" and that sanctions are "ineffective" and "harm France's interests more than Russia's."
  • In 2020–2021, RN deputies regularly abstained from voting on key resolutions condemning repression in Russia, including resolutions regarding the poisoning of Alexei Navaly and the suppression of civil society.

The situation after 2022 — intensifying ambiguity

Following the start of the full-scale war, the party formally condemned the aggression, but in practice, voting records looked different:

  • The French publication franceinfo noted that while in words Marine Le Pen condemns Russian aggression, in reality the National Rally has refused to support the majority of financial and military aid intended for Kyiv.
  • Another publication by the same outlet emphasized the inconsistency: it is difficult to say what Marine Le Pen would do if she came to power. One day the National Rally declares unwavering support for Ukraine, and the next day the party announces its desire to cut France's aid.

Recent events (September 2026)

Literally in recent weeks, the party has made a noticeable pivot in its rhetoric:

  • On September 19, 2026, Marine Le Pen and Jordan Bardella took a firm stance regarding Moscow; at the same time, Le Pen, who is leading in the presidential race polls, regularly faces criticism from opponents for positions considered conciliatory toward Putin.
  • In a statement where a pro-Russian stance was previously often perceived, RN leaders sharply condemned the Kremlin's actions—specifically, Putin's decision to place the assets of the Russian subsidiaries of Auchan, Nestle, and Leroy Merlin under "temporary management," as well as Russia's hybrid attacks on Europe.
  • The reaction from Moscow was sharp: according to Dmitry Medvedev, this political force is currently exhibiting behavior characteristic of a typical Russophobic European party.
  • At the same time, Le Pen herself continues to call for restraint: in a recent interview with Politico, she warned against provocations directed at Russia, noting that some talk easily about war with Russia, even though such statements should be avoided.
  • For the first time since 2022, an RN representative, Laurent Jacobelli, visited Kyiv—he arrived in Ukraine on September 21, marking the first visit by a prominent RN deputy to Kyiv since the Russian invasion in 2022, although the visit was formally not direct support from the RN candidate, but rather part of a parliamentary mission on European defense.

Conclusion

Skeptics and RN opponents view this sharp pivot ahead of the 2027 presidential election as a tactical move: the French newspaper Le Monde calls it a sort of "brinkmanship" ahead of the election, while Le Pen herself, according to the publication, is still forced to make excuses for her "long-standing, but strong" fascination with Vladimir Putin.

Thus: historically and through many practical votes, the RN has indeed demonstrated pro-Russian sympathies (skepticism toward NATO, sanctions, support for Ukraine, and financial ties with a Russian bank), but in recent weeks the party has sharply hardened its public rhetoric against Moscow—which many observers interpret as a pre-election tactical move rather than a definitive ideological break.

Original text

Однозначного ответа «да» или «нет» тут дать нельзя — картина неоднозначная и со временем менялась. Вот ключевые факты.

Исторические связи с Россией

У «Национального объединения» (RN, ранее «Национальный фронт») действительно есть исторический шлейф пророссийских позиций:

  • Более десяти лет назад «Национальное объединение», тогда еще «Национальный фронт», подписало кредит на 9,4 млн евро с ныне не существующим Первым чешско-российским банком, и, по данным Le Monde, RN не погасило этот кредит досрочно и не рефинансировало его через французские институты, хотя финансовая возможность появилась.
  • По мере того как европейский климат сдвигался к состоянию войны, RN оставалась идеологически на прежних позициях: скептически относилась к НАТО, с осторожностью — к санкциям и с почтением — к Москве. Незадолго до полномасштабного вторжения России в Украину Марин Ле Пен утверждала, что «НАТО следует распустить», а санкции «неэффективны» и «вредят интересам Франции больше, чем России».
  • В 2020–2021 годах депутаты RN регулярно уклонялись от голосования по ключевым резолюциям, осуждающим репрессии в России, включая резолюции по отравлению Алексея Навального и по подавлению гражданского общества.

Ситуация после 2022 года — усиливающаяся двойственность

После начала полномасштабной войны партия формально осудила агрессию, но на практике голосования выглядели иначе:

  • Французское издание franceinfo отмечало: хотя на словах Марин Ле Пен осуждает российскую агрессию, на деле «Национальное объединение» отказалось поддержать большинство финансовой и военной помощи, предназначенной для Киева.
  • В другой публикации того же издания подчёркивалась непоследовательность: трудно сказать, что стала бы делать Марин Ле Пен, придя к власти. Один день «Национальное объединение» заявляет о непоколебимой поддержке Украины, на следующий — партия объявляет о желании сократить помощь Франции.

Последние события (сентябрь 2026)

Буквально в последние недели партия сделала заметный разворот в риторике:

  • 19 сентября 2026 года Марин Ле Пен и Жордан Барделла заняли твёрдую позицию в отношении Москвы; при этом Ле Пен, лидирующая в опросах президентской гонки, регулярно подвергается критике со стороны оппонентов за позиции, считающиеся примирительными по отношению к Путину.
  • В заявлении, где ранее часто усматривалась пророссийская позиция, лидеры RN резко осудили действия Кремля — в частности, решение Путина передать под «временное управление» активы российских «дочек» компаний Ашан, Nestle и Leroy Merlin, а также гибридные атаки РФ на Европу.
  • Реакция из Москвы была резкой: по словам Дмитрия Медведева, эта политическая сила в настоящий момент демонстрирует поведение, характерное для типичной русофобской европейской партии.
  • При этом сама Ле Пен продолжает призывать к сдержанности: в недавнем интервью Politico она предостерегла от провокаций в адрес России, отметив, что некоторые легко говорят о войне с Россией, хотя от подобных заявлений следует воздерживаться.
  • Впервые с 2022 года представитель RN Лоран Жакобелли посетил Киев — он прибыл в Украину 21 сентября, и это первый визит видного депутата RN в Киев с момента российского вторжения в 2022 году, хотя формально визит не был прямой поддержкой от кандидата RN, а частью парламентской миссии по европейской обороне.

Вывод

Скептики и оппоненты RN считают этот резкий разворот перед президентскими выборами 2027 года тактическим ходом: французская газета Le Monde называет это своеобразным «балансированием на грани» в преддверии выборов, а сама Ле Пен, по мнению издания, до сих пор вынуждена оправдываться за своё «давнее, но сильное» увлечение Владимиром Путиным.

Таким образом: исторически и по многим практическим голосованиям RN действительно демонстрировало пророссийские симпатии (скепсис к НАТО, санкциям, поддержке Украины, финансовые связи с российским банком), но в последние недели партия резко ужесточила публичную риторику против Москвы — что многие наблюдатели интерпретируют как предвыборный тактический шаг, а не как окончательный идеологический разрыв.

English translation

Is the National Rally a pro-Russian party?

This is a nuanced question, and the answer to it changes depending on the temporal context. The situation here is quite dynamic.

Historically — yes, the party had close ties with Russia

More than a decade ago, the National Rally (then still the National Front) signed a €9.4 million loan with the now-defunct First Czech-Russian Bank. This history of financial ties still resurfaces from time to time in debates.

In addition, in 2015, Marine Le Pen described Russian President Vladimir Putin as "the defender of the Christian heritage of European civilization." The party also long held the position that Ukraine had been subjugated by the United States through the events of 2014.

In her platform for the 2022 presidential election, Le Pen proposed limiting military support for Ukraine and voting against new sanctions on Russia, withdrawing from NATO's integrated command, and resuming "strategic rapprochement between NATO and Russia" as soon as peace between Moscow and Kyiv is secured.

But the situation has changed significantly

As the war in Ukraine continued, the party had to adjust its rhetoric. Analysts note that Le Pen had to soften her pro-Russian rhetoric to stay closer to French public opinion, yet she continues to promote a foreign policy that diverges from the mainstream course of French and European policy.

A sharp turn in September 2026

The most recent events show a clear reversal. On September 19, 2026, Marine Le Pen and Jordan Bardella took a firm stance against Moscow in a special statement. The occasion was the decision by Russian President Vladimir Putin to transfer the assets of the Russian subsidiaries of Auchan, Nestle, and Leroy Merlin to "temporary management," as well as Russia's hybrid attacks on Europe.

Le Monde notes that never before has the National Rally spoken out so directly and distanced itself so strongly from Russia. At the same time, the publication characterizes this as a statement made with an eye on the election campaign—the party is distancing itself from Russia without clarifying its position on Ukraine, which is a kind of "tightrope walking" ahead of the 2027 presidential election.

The Russian side reacted harshly: Dmitry Medvedev, Deputy Chairman of the Security Council of the Russian Federation, stated that the French National Rally party, led by Marine Le Pen, has come to adhere to an anti-Russian position, noting at the same time that the National Rally was previously perceived as a force holding a more balanced position on a number of international issues, but now, in his view, the party fits into the general anti-Russian trend that dominates Europe's political establishment, even though for a long time it positioned itself as an alternative to mainstream forces by criticizing NATO and EU policies.

But not everything is so clear-cut

Even within the right-wing camp, there is no consensus on the change that has taken place. Former Le Pen ally Nicolas Dupont-Aignan believes that the National Rally's position regarding Russia is "unclear" and that he "does not recognize" it when it comes to Russia.

Moreover, just the other day (September 25, 2026), Le Pen softened her tone again: in an interview with Politico, she warned against provocations toward Russia, noting that some speak lightly of war with Russia, but such statements should be avoided.

Conclusion

A definitive "yes" or "no" cannot be given. The party has a long history of ties with Russia and pro-Russian rhetoric, which even independent analysts acknowledge, calling it a "strategic alliance." However, in recent weeks, ahead of the 2027 presidential election, the party has sharply toughened its public rhetoric regarding Moscow—which many observers interpret as electoral tactics rather than a sincere change of convictions. At the same time, Le Pen continues to make statements toward Russia that are conciliatory in other contexts, creating the impression of a fluctuating rather than consistent position.

Original text

Является ли «Национальное объединение» пророссийской партией?

Это неоднозначный вопрос, и ответ на него меняется в зависимости от временного контекста. Ситуация здесь довольно динамичная.

Исторически — да, у партии были тесные связи с Россией

Более десяти лет назад Национальное объединение (тогда еще Национальный фронт) подписало кредит на 9,4 млн евро с ныне не существующим «Первым чешско-российским банком». Эта история финансовых связей до сих пор время от времени всплывает в дебатах.

Кроме того, в 2015 году Марин Ле Пен описывала президента России Владимира Путина как «защитника христианского наследия европейской цивилизации». Партия также долгое время придерживалась позиции, что Украина была подчинена Соединёнными Штатами через события 2014 года.

В программе на президентских выборах 2022 года Ле Пен предлагала ограничить военную поддержку Украины и не голосовать за новые санкции против России, выйти из объединённого командования НАТО и возобновить «стратегическое сближение между НАТО и Россией», как только будет обеспечен мир между Москвой и Киевом.

Но ситуация значительно изменилась

По мере продолжения войны в Украине партии пришлось скорректировать риторику. Аналитики отмечают, что Ле Пен пришлось смягчить свою пророссийскую риторику, чтобы оставаться ближе к французскому общественному мнению, однако она продолжает продвигать внешнюю политику, которая расходится с основным курсом французской и европейской политики.

Резкий поворот в сентябре 2026

Самые свежие события показывают явный разворот. 19 сентября 2026 года Марин Ле Пен и Жордан Барделла заняли твёрдую позицию в отношении Москвы в специальном заявлении. Поводом стало решение российского президента Владимира Путина передать во «временное управление» активы российских «дочек» компаний «Ашан», Nestle и Leroy Merlin, а также гибридные атаки РФ на Европу.

Le Monde отмечает, что никогда прежде партия «Национальное объединение» так прямо не высказывалась и так сильно не дистанцировалась от России. При этом издание характеризует это как заявление, сделанное с прицелом на предвыборную кампанию — партия дистанцируется от России, не уточняя свою позицию по Украине, что является своего рода «балансированием на грани» перед президентскими выборами 2027 года.

Российская сторона отреагировала жёстко: заместитель председателя Совбеза РФ Дмитрий Медведев заявил, что французская партия «Национальное объединение» во главе с Марин Ле Пен стала придерживаться антироссийской позиции, отметив при этом, что «Национальное объединение» ранее воспринималось как сила, занимающая более взвешенную позицию по ряду международных вопросов, однако сейчас, по его мнению, партия вписывается в общий антироссийский тренд, который доминирует в политическом истеблишменте Европы, хотя долгое время она позиционировала себя как альтернатива мейнстримным силам, критикуя политику НАТО и ЕС.

Но не всё так однозначно

Даже внутри правого лагеря нет единого мнения о произошедшей перемене. Бывший союзник Ле Пен Николя Дюпон-Эньян считает, что позиция «Национального объединения» в отношении России «непонятна», и что он «не узнаёт» её, когда речь заходит о России.

Более того, буквально на днях (25 сентября 2026) Ле Пен вновь смягчила тон: в интервью Politico она предостерегла от провокаций в адрес России, отметив, что некоторые с легкостью говорят о войне с Россией, но от подобных заявлений следует воздерживаться.

Вывод

Однозначного ответа «да» или «нет» дать нельзя. Партия имеет долгую историю связей с Россией и пророссийской риторики, что признают даже независимые аналитики, называющие это «стратегическим альянсом». Однако в последние недели, накануне президентских выборов 2027 года, партия резко ужесточила публичную риторику в отношении Москвы — что многие наблюдатели интерпретируют как предвыборную тактику, а не искреннюю смену убеждений. Одновременно Ле Пен продолжает делать примирительные по отношению к России заявления в других контекстах, что создаёт впечатление колеблющейся, а не последовательной позиции.

English translation

Is the National Rally a pro-Russian party?

This is a complex and controversial question, the answer to which depends on the time period and context. The situation is ambiguous and evolving.

Historical connection with Russia

The party genuinely has long-standing ties with Moscow. The ties between the National Rally (known as the National Front until 2018) and Russia have a long history, and Marine Le Pen winning the presidential election would obviously be good news for Russia. The party took out a €9.4 million loan from the now-defunct First Czech-Russian Bank, and the consequences of that decision continue to reverberate within the party.

Ideologically, the party long adhered to a pro-Russian course: as Europe leaned increasingly toward war, the National Rally remained ideologically unchanged, maintaining a skepticism toward NATO, fearing sanctions, and showing deference toward Moscow. Shortly before Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine, Marine Le Pen continued to insist that "NATO should be dissolved" and that sanctions are "ineffective" and "cause more harm to French interests than to Russian ones." Additionally, in 2020–2021, party deputies regularly abstained from voting on key resolutions condemning repression in Russia, including the poisoning cases of Alexei Navalny.

Even during the 2022 presidential campaign, Le Pen stated that she would like to see Russia as an ally of France, and attributed the tensions between Moscow and the West to NATO's military pressure. Moreover, she said she completely disbelieved in Russia's intention to invade Ukraine—this was said shortly before the full-scale war began.

Gradual evolution of the position

After the start of the war, the party began to soften its rhetoric, although it maintained a distinct stance. Against the backdrop of the war in Ukraine, Le Pen had to soften her pro-Russian rhetoric in order to stay closer to French public opinion; however, she continues to promote a foreign policy that runs counter to the majority of French and European policy.

By 2024, Le Pen publicly stated: "We no longer have anything in common with Russia," although experts noted parallels with the situation in Slovakia, where Prime Minister Robert Fico ran a pro-European campaign and then sharply changed course after being elected.

Sharp turn in September 2025

The most noticeable change occurred quite recently. After Russia placed the assets of French companies Auchan, Nestlé, and Leroy Merlin under "temporary management," and also against the backdrop of hybrid attacks on Europe, Le Pen and party leader Jordan Bardella issued an unprecedentedly harsh statement. As Le Monde notes, never before had the National Rally spoken out so directly and distanced itself so strongly from Russia.

In a joint statement, they emphasized: "No provocation, no covert operation, and no destabilizing actions must force France to assist Russia's military goals in Ukraine." They also stated that Russia cannot directly or indirectly dictate French policy, while sharply criticizing Moscow's hostile actions.

The Russian side reacted extremely negatively—Dmitry Medvedev, Deputy Chairman of the Security Council of the Russian Federation, stated that the French National Rally party led by Marine Le Pen had begun to adopt an anti-Russian position, calling it a manifestation of Russophobia.

Nuances and caveats

At the same time, it is important to understand that a harsh tone regarding Russia's "hostile actions" does not mean full support for Ukraine. Le Monde notes that Marine Le Pen's party is distancing itself from Russia without clarifying its position on Ukraine, describing this as tightrope walking ahead of the 2027 presidential election.

The practical position on military aid to Ukraine remains limited: Le Pen expressed readiness to supply military equipment to strengthen Kyiv's defense, but ruled out providing additional financial aid.

Conclusion

Historically and by the nature of its ideology—yes, the National Rally long demonstrated a pro-Russian orientation: skepticism toward NATO, opposition to sanctions, financial ties with Russian structures, and almost friendly rhetoric toward Putin prior to 2022.

To date (September 2025 – September 2026)—the party is undergoing a noticeable transformation, publicly distancing itself from the Kremlin and harshly condemning Russia's hostile actions on European soil. However, critics (including Ukrainian experts) consider this to be pre-election tactics ahead of the 2027 elections rather than a fundamental ideological shift, since the specific position on the scale of support for Ukraine remains vague and limited.

Thus, a straightforward "yes" or "no" cannot be given—the party is evolving from a clearly pro-Russian position to a more restrained, yet not pro-Ukrainian position, balancing between historical ties and wartime electoral realities.

Original text

Является ли «Национальное объединение» пророссийской партией?

Это сложный и спорный вопрос, ответ на который зависит от временного периода и контекста. Ситуация неоднозначная и эволюционирует.

Историческая связь с Россией

У партии действительно есть давние связи с Москвой. Связи «Национального объединения» (до 2018 года — «Национальный фронт») с Россией имеют давнюю историю, и победа Марин Ле Пен на президентских выборах была бы очевидно хорошей новостью для России. Партия подписала кредит на 9,4 млн евро с ныне не существующим Первым чешско-российским банком, и последствия этого решения продолжают отражаться на партии.

Идеологически партия долгое время придерживалась пророссийского курса: по мере того как Европа всё сильнее склонялась к войне, «Национальное объединение» оставалось идеологически неизменным — скептически относилось к НАТО, опасалось санкций и проявляло почтительность к Москве. Незадолго до полномасштабного вторжения России в Украину Марин Ле Пен продолжала настаивать, что «НАТО следует распустить», а санкции «неэффективны» и «наносят больший вред французским интересам, чем российским». Также в 2020–2021 годах депутаты от партии регулярно уклонялись от голосования по ключевым резолюциям, осуждающим репрессии в России, включая дела об отравлении Алексея Навального.

Даже во время президентской кампании 2022 года Ле Пен заявляла: она хотела бы видеть Россию союзником Франции, а напряжённость между Москвой и Западом объясняла военным давлением НАТО. Более того, она говорила, что совершенно не верит в намерение России вторгнуться в Украину — это было сказано незадолго до начала полномасштабной войны.

Постепенная эволюция позиции

После начала войны партия начала смягчать риторику, хотя сохраняла особую позицию. На фоне войны в Украине Ле Пен пришлось смягчить свою пророссийскую риторику, чтобы оставаться ближе к французскому общественному мнению, однако она продолжает продвигать внешнюю политику, идущую вразрез с большинством французской и европейской политики.

К 2024 году Ле Пен публично заявляла: «У нас больше нет ничего общего с Россией», хотя эксперты отмечали параллели с ситуацией в Словакии, где премьер Роберт Фицо провёл проевропейскую кампанию, а затем резко изменил курс после избрания.

Резкий поворот в сентябре 2025 года

Наиболее заметное изменение произошло совсем недавно. После того как Россия передала под "временное управление" активы французских компаний «Ашан», Nestlé и Leroy Merlin, а также на фоне гибридных атак на Европу, Ле Пен и лидер партии Жордан Барделла выступили с беспрецедентно резким заявлением. Как отмечает Le Monde, никогда прежде партия «Национальное объединение» так прямо не высказывалась и так сильно не дистанцировалась от России.

В совместном заявлении они подчеркнули: «Никакая провокация, никакая скрытая операция и никакие дестабилизирующие действия не должны заставить Францию содействовать военным целям России в Украине». Также они заявили, что Россия не может прямо или косвенно диктовать французскую политику, при этом резко критикуя враждебные действия Москвы.

Российская сторона отреагировала крайне негативно — заместитель председателя Совбеза РФ Дмитрий Медведев заявил, что французская партия «Национальное объединение» во главе с Марин Ле Пен стала придерживаться антироссийской позиции, назвав это проявлением русофобии.

Нюансы и оговорки

При этом важно понимать, что резкий тон в отношении «враждебных действий» России не означает полной поддержки Украины. Le Monde отмечает, что партия Марин Ле Пен дистанцируется от России, не уточняя при этом свою позицию по Украине, называя это балансированием на грани перед президентскими выборами 2027 года.

Практическая позиция по военной помощи Украине остаётся ограниченной: Ле Пен выразила готовность поставлять военную технику для укрепления обороны Киева, однако исключила предоставление дополнительной финансовой помощи.

Вывод

Исторически и по сути идеологии — да, «Национальное объединение» долгое время демонстрировало пророссийскую ориентацию: скептицизм к НАТО, противодействие санкциям, финансовые связи с российскими структурами и почти дружественная риторика по отношению к Путину до 2022 года.

На сегодняшний день (сентябрь 2025 – сентябрь 2026) — партия проходит через заметную трансформацию, публично дистанцируясь от Кремля и жёстко осуждая враждебные действия России на европейской территории. Однако критики (в том числе украинские эксперты) считают это предвыборной тактикой перед выборами 2027 года, а не фундаментальным идеологическим сдвигом, поскольку конкретная позиция по масштабу поддержки Украины остаётся размытой и ограниченной.

Таким образом, однозначного ответа "да" или "нет" дать нельзя — партия эволюционирует от явно пророссийской к более сдержанной, но не проукраинской позиции, балансируя между историческими связями и электоральными реалиями военного времени.

English translation

This is a complex and debatable question, so let me provide up-to-date information from various sources.## There is no simple answer — the situation is complex and changing

Historically, the National Rally (formerly the National Front) had close ties with Russia, but recently the party has significantly distanced itself from the Kremlin.

Historical ties with Russia

The ties between the National Rally (until 2018 the National Front) and Russia have a long history. Marine Le Pen outlined foreign policy priorities: to limit military support for Ukraine, avoid voting for new sanctions against Russia, withdraw from the NATO joint command, and restore a "strategic rapprochement between NATO and Russia."

The party also previously took a more pro-Russian stance, and Le Pen met with Russian President Vladimir Putin. The party also took a €9.4 million loan from a Czech-Russian bank associated with the Kremlin, which it later repaid.

Significant shift in position in 2025-2026

However, the party has recently radically changed its rhetoric. Back in September 2025, the party repaid the loan to the Czech-Russian bank, fully paying out €6.1 million of the original €9.4 million to the Russian firm Aviazachast.</cite>

Most importantly, in September 2026, the party issued a joint statement condemning Russia. <cite index="13-1,13-3,13-4">National Rally leaders Jordan Bardella and Marine Le Pen issued a joint statement condemning Russia's actions as an aggressive state, stating that they would not tolerate attempts by a foreign power to influence French politics through "intimidation, threats, destabilization, or psychological pressure."

This was a reaction to specific actions by Russia: Bardella and Le Pen also mentioned Russia's seizure of assets of companies that continued operating in Russia, including the French retailer Auchan and the Swiss company Nestlé, calling it a "hostile act against the interests of France and European countries."

This statement was unusual for the party, as there were internal divisions within it over whether to consider the Kremlin an enemy — Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine in 2022 exposed these contradictions.

Reaction from Moscow

The Kremlin perceived this change extremely negatively. Deputy Chairman of the Russian Security Council Dmitry Medvedev accused the National Rally and its leader Marine Le Pen of "russophobia" and of turning into an "ordinary European party," as tensions between Moscow and the party, often called one of the most "pro-Putin" political forces in the EU, are increasing.

Difference in leaders' positions

Interestingly, there is a generational split within the party: Le Pen previously took a more pro-Russian stance, while Bardella portrayed Moscow as a threat to France and Europe</cite>. At the same time, <cite index="15-5,15-6">Jordan Bardella represents a younger political generation whose formative experiences include Islamist terrorism, mass migration, the Russian invasion of Ukraine, and the resurgence of large-scale interstate war in Europe — his political nationalism does not necessarily lead to the same geopolitical conclusions as the older Gaullist tradition of suspicion toward Washington, NATO, and European integration.

Conclusion

Therefore, directly defining the National Rally as a "pro-Putin" or "pro-Russian" party is currently inaccurate. It would be mistaken to describe the modern Marine Le Pen simply as pro-Putin. The party has a complex history of ties with Russia and Kremlin-sympathetic rhetoric, but since 2022, and especially in September 2026, it has taken an open and public step toward condemning Russian aggression and interference in French politics — while maintaining a cautious position regarding additional military or financial aid to Ukraine.

Original text

Це складне і дискусійне питання, тому дозвольте навести актуальну інформацію з різних джерел.## Однозначної відповіді немає — ситуація складна та змінюється

Історично «Національне об'єднання» (колишній «Національний фронт») мало тісні зв'язки з Росією, але останнім часом партія суттєво дистанціювалася від Кремля.

Історичні зв'язки з Росією

Зв'язки «Національного об'єднання» (до 2018 року — «Національний фронт») з Росією мають давню історію. Марін Ле Пен окреслювала пріоритети зовнішньої політики: обмежити військову підтримку Україні, уникати голосування за нові санкції проти Росії, вийти з об'єднаного командування НАТО та відновити «стратегічне наближення між НАТО та Росією».

Партія також раніше займала більш проросійську позицію, а Ле Пен зустрічалася з президентом Росії Володимиром Путіним. Партія також взяла кредит на 9,4 млн євро у чесько-російського банку, пов'язаного з Кремлем, який пізніше повернула.

Суттєва зміна позиції у 2025-2026 роках

Проте останнім часом партія кардинально змінила риторику. Ще у вересні 2025 року партія повернула кредит чесько-російському банку, повністю виплативши 6,1 млн євро з початкових 9,4 млн росій��ській фірмі Aviazachast</cite.

Найважливіше — у вересні 2026 року партія випустила спільну заяву, яка засуджує Росію. <cite index="13-1,13-3,13-4">Лідери «Національного об'єднання» Жордан Барделла та Марін Ле Пен випустили спільну заяву, засуджуючи дії Росії як агресивної держави, заявивши, що не толеруватимуть спроб іноземної держави впливати на французьку політику через «залякування, погрози, дестабілізацію чи психологічний тиск».

Це стало реакцією на конкретні дії Росії: Барделла та Ле Пен також згадали захоплення Росією активів компаній, що продовжували працювати в Росії, включно з французьким ритейлером Auchan та швейцарською компанією Nestlé, назвавши це «ворожим актом проти інтересів Франції та європейських країн».

Ця заява стала незвичною для партії, оскільки всередині неї були розбіжності щодо того, чи вважати Кремль ворогом — повномасштабне вторгнення Росії в Україну у 2022 році оголило ці протиріччя.

Реакція з Москви

Кремль сприйняв цю зміну вкрай негативно. Заступник голови Ради безпеки Росії Дмитро Медведєв звинуватив «Національне об'єднання» та його лідерку Марін Ле Пен у «русофобії» та у перетворенні на «звичайну європейську партію», оскільки напруга між Москвою та партією, яку часто називають однією з найбільш «пропутінських» політичних сил в ЄС, посилюється.

Різниця в позиціях лідерів

Цікаво, що всередині партії існує розкол за поколіннями: Ле Пен раніше займала більш прихильну до Росії позицію, тоді як Барделла зображував Москву як загрозу для Франції та Європи</cite. При цьому <cite index="15-5,15-6">Жордан Барделла представляє молодше політичне покоління, чий формуючий досвід включає ісламістський тероризм, масову міграцію, російське вторгнення в Україну та відродження масштабної міждержавної війни в Європі — його політичний націоналізм не обов'язково призводить до тих самих геополітичних висновків, що й старша голлістська традиція підозри щодо Вашингтона, НАТО та європейської інтеграції.

Висновок

Тому пряме визначення «Національного об'єднання» як «пропутінської» чи «проросійської» партії наразі є неточним. Було б помилковим описувати сучасну Марін Ле Пен просто як пропутінську. Партія має складну історію зв'язків з Росією та риторики, симпатичної Кремлю, але з 2022 року, а особливо у вересні 2026 року, вона зробила відкритий і публічний крок у бік засудження російської агресії та втручання в французьку політику — хоча зберігає позицію, обмежену щодо додаткової військової чи фінансової допомоги Україні.
