English translation

The answer to this question has two dimensions: the official position of the party itself (which tries to distance itself from this label) and the actual activity, voting record, and history of ties, through which political scientists, investigative journalists, and the French parliament view the National Rally (RN) as a force acting in Moscow's interests.

Below is a detailed overview of why Marine Le Pen's party is considered pro-Russian and how its position has evolved.

1. Facts Indicating a Pro-Russian Orientation

  • Financial dependence on the Russian Federation:
    In 2014, the party took out a 9.4 million euro loan from the First Czech-Russian Bank, which is linked to the Kremlin. The party only finished paying off this debt in the autumn of 2023. In addition, another foundation associated with Marine Le Pen's father, Jean-Marie Le Pen, also received Russian loans.

  • Support for the annexation of Crimea and visits to Moscow:
    Marine Le Pen has repeatedly stated publicly that she considers Russia's 2014 annexation of Crimea "legitimate," arguing this based on a pseudo-referendum. In March 2017, a month before the French presidential election, she visited Moscow and personally met with Vladimir Putin in the Kremlin.

  • Conclusion of the French Parliamentary Commission (2023):
    In June 2023, the French National Assembly published an investigative report on foreign interference. In it, the National Rally was explicitly described as a "transmission belt" (courroie de transmission) for broadcasting Russian influence and Kremlin narratives in France.

  • Ideological affinity:
    For years, the party has opposed "US hegemony," called for France's withdrawal from NATO's integrated military command, criticized the European Union, and promoted the concept of a "greater Europe from Lisbon to Vladivostok," which envisioned a strategic alliance with Moscow.

2. Change in Rhetoric After the Full-Scale Invasion (2022)

After February 24, 2022, the party—led by Marine Le Pen and the current party president Jordan Bardella—was forced to adjust its rhetoric as part of its strategy of normalization (dédiabolisation):

  • They condemned the direct military aggression of the Russian Federation against Ukraine and the violation of international law.
  • They declared support for Ukraine's sovereignty and the provision of humanitarian aid.
  • During the 2024 parliamentary elections, Bardella emphasized that he would not allow Russia to conquer Ukraine, recognizing the Russian Federation as a threat to European security.

3. Realpolitik and Voting Record on Ukraine

Despite verbal condemnations of the aggression, in practice the party's voting record and programmatic statements remain advantageous to Moscow:

  • Opposition to military assistance: The RN systematically opposes providing Ukraine with long-range weapons (such as SCALP missiles), citing the risk of "escalation" and a direct clash with nuclear-armed Russia.
  • Parliamentary votes: RN deputies in the European Parliament and the National Assembly have repeatedly voted against or abstained during the adoption of resolutions on financial and military support for Ukraine, as well as the bilateral security agreement between France and Ukraine.
  • Sanctions: The party opposes energy sanctions against the Russian Federation, arguing that they harm the purchasing power of the French people through rising prices.
  • NATO and the EU: Le Pen and Bardella are categorically opposed to Ukraine's accession to the European Union and NATO, as well as to the deployment of any French military instructors to the territory of Ukraine.

Conclusion

Officially, the National Rally rejects the label of a "pro-Russian party," calling itself exclusively "pro-French" and "sovereignist."

However, the combination of factors—long-standing financial and political ties with the Kremlin, consistent sabotage of effective aid to Ukraine, resistance to anti-Russian sanctions, and the weakening of NATO—gives the majority of analysts, political commentators, and international observers grounds to classify the RN as a party whose foreign policy line objectively plays into the interests of the Kremlin.

Відповідь на це запитання має дві площини: офіційну позицію самої партії (яка намагається дистанціюватися від цього ярлика) та фактичну діяльність, голосування й історію зв'язків, через які політологи, журналісти-розслідувачі та французький парламент розглядають «Національне об’єднання» (RN) як силу, що діє в інтересах Москви.

Нижче наведено детальний огляд того, чому партію Марін Ле Пен вважають проросійською і як змінювалася її позиція.

1. Факти, що свідчать про проросійську орієнтацію

  • Фінансова залежність від РФ:
    У 2014 році партія взяла кредит у розмірі 9,4 мільйона євро в Першому чесько-російському банку (First Czech Russian Bank), пов’язаному з Кремлем. Виплату цього боргу партія закрила лише восени 2023 року. Крім того, інший фонд, пов’язаний із батьком Марін Ле Пен — Жаном-Марі Ле Пеном, також отримував російські позики.

  • Підтримка анексії Криму та візити до Москви:
    Марін Ле Пен неодноразово публічно заявляла, що вважає анексію Криму Росією у 2014 році «законною», аргументуючи це псевдореферендумом. У березні 2017 року, за місяць до президентських виборів у Франції, вона відвідала Москву і особисто зустрілася з Володимиром Путіним у Кремлі.

  • Висновок парламентської комісії Франції (2023 рік):
    У червні 2023 року Національна асамблея Франції опублікувала звіт розслідування щодо іноземного втручання. У ньому «Національне об'єднання» було прямо названо «привідним пасом» (courroie de transmission) для трансляції російського впливу та кремлівських наративів у Франції.

  • Ідеологічна спорідненість:
    Роками партія виступала проти «гегемонії США», закликала до виходу Франції з об'єднаного військового командування НАТО, критикувала Європейський Союз і просувала концепцію «великої Європи від Лісабона до Владивостока», яка передбачала стратегічний союз із Москвою.

2. Зміна риторики після повномасштабного вторгнення (2022 рік)

Після 24 лютого 2022 року партія на чолі з Марін Ле Пен та чинним президентом партії Жорданом Барделла була змушена скоригувати риторику в межах своєї стратегії респектабелізації (dédiabolisation):

  • Вони засудили пряму військову агресію РФ проти України та порушення міжнародного права.
  • Заявили про підтримку суверенітету України та надання гуманітарної допомоги.
  • Барделла під час парламентських виборів 2024 року підкреслював, що не дозволить Росії захопити Україну, визнаючи РФ загрозою європейській безпеці.

3. Реальна політика та голосування щодо України

Попри засудження агресії на словах, на практиці голосування та програмні заяви партії залишаються вигідними Москві:

  • Протидія військовій допомозі: RN системно виступає проти надання Україні далекобійної зброї (наприклад, ракет SCALP), мотивуючи це ризиком «ескалації» та прямого зіткнення з ядерною Росією.
  • Голосування в парламентах: Депутати RN у Європарламенті та Національній асамблеї неодноразово голосували проти або утримувалися під час ухвалення резолюцій про фінансову та військову підтримку України, а також двосторонньої безпекової угоди між Францією та Україною.
  • Санкції: Партія виступає проти енергетичних санкцій щодо РФ, стверджуючи, що вони шкодять купівельній спроможності французів через зростання цін.
  • НАТО та ЄС: Ле Пен і Барделла категорично виступають проти вступу України до Євросоюзу та НАТО, а також проти відправлення будь-яких французьких військових інструкторів на територію України.

Висновок

Офіційно «Національне об'єднання» відкидає ярлик «проросійської партії», називаючи себе виключно «профранцузькою» та «сувереністською».

Проте сукупність факторів — багаторічні фінансові та політичні зв'язки з Кремлем, послідовне саботування дієвої допомоги Україні, опір антиросійським санкціям та послаблення НАТО — дає підстави більшості аналітиків, політичних опонентів та міжнародних оглядачів класифікувати RN як партію, чия зовнішньополітична лінія об'єктивно підігрує інтересам Кремля.

English — answer round 1

Pages the model opened: - Drawing Ukraine red lines, French far right talks tough on Russia - Has France's far-right National Rally really turned on Russia? - RFI - Russia cheers Marine Le Pen’s National Rally in French election – POLITICO - Marine Le Pen promises French far right will rein in aid to Ukraine, slams soccer star Mbappé | CNN - French far right pulls manifesto that included controversial Russia, NATO plans – POLITICO

English translation

France's National Rally (Rassemblement National, RN), the party of Marine Le Pen, has long been accused of having a pro-Russian stance, and there is substantial evidence supporting this characterization. The party has historical and financial ties to Russia: it received a €9 million loan from a Russian bank in 2014, which was only fully repaid in 2023. Marine Le Pen herself has visited Moscow multiple times and has expressed admiration for Vladimir Putin. The RN has consistently opposed EU sanctions against Russia for its invasion of Ukraine and, in its 2022 presidential program, advocated for deepening diplomatic ties with Moscow and even sought an alliance with Russia on certain issues. The party also proposed that France leave NATO’s integrated military command, a position it has only recently toned down as it seeks to appear more moderate ahead of elections.

However, in the lead-up to the 2024 parliamentary elections, the National Rally has attempted to soften its image, removing some of its most controversial Russia-related proposals from its manifesto and pledging not to escalate tensions with Russia or become a "co-belligerent" in the Ukraine conflict. Despite these shifts, the party has never voted in favor of a resolution supporting Ukraine or condemning Russian aggression in either the French or European parliaments. Russia has also openly expressed support for Le Pen’s party, further underscoring the historical alignment between the RN and Moscow.

In summary, while the National Rally has recently tried to moderate its stance, its historical positions, financial ties, and ongoing voting record strongly suggest that it has been, and remains, a pro-Russian party.

Original text

France's National Rally (Rassemblement National, RN), the party of Marine Le Pen, has long been accused of having a pro-Russian stance, and there is substantial evidence supporting this characterization. The party has historical and financial ties to Russia: it received a €9 million loan from a Russian bank in 2014, which was only fully repaid in 2023. Marine Le Pen herself has visited Moscow multiple times and has expressed admiration for Vladimir Putin. The RN has consistently opposed EU sanctions against Russia for its invasion of Ukraine and, in its 2022 presidential program, advocated for deepening diplomatic ties with Moscow and even sought an alliance with Russia on certain issues. The party also proposed that France leave NATO’s integrated military command, a position it has only recently toned down as it seeks to appear more moderate ahead of elections.

However, in the lead-up to the 2024 parliamentary elections, the National Rally has attempted to soften its image, removing some of its most controversial Russia-related proposals from its manifesto and pledging not to escalate tensions with Russia or become a "co-belligerent" in the Ukraine conflict. Despite these shifts, the party has never voted in favor of a resolution supporting Ukraine or condemning Russian aggression in either the French or European parliaments. Russia has also openly expressed support for Le Pen’s party, further underscoring the historical alignment between the RN and Moscow.

In summary, while the National Rally has recently tried to moderate its stance, its historical positions, financial ties, and ongoing voting record strongly suggest that it has been, and remains, a pro-Russian party.

English translation

France's National Rally (Rassemblement National, RN), the party of Marine Le Pen, has long been accused of having a pro-Russian stance, and there is substantial evidence supporting this characterization. The party has historical and financial ties to Russia: in 2014, RN took out a €9 million loan from a Russian bank, which was only fully repaid in 2023. Marine Le Pen herself has visited Moscow multiple times, expressed admiration for Vladimir Putin, and in her 2022 presidential program, she advocated for deepening diplomatic ties with Russia and even sought "an alliance with Russia on certain issues" like European security. The party also opposed EU sanctions against Russia following its invasion of Ukraine and, until recently, called for France to leave NATO’s integrated military command.

However, as the National Rally has sought to moderate its image and gain broader electoral appeal, especially ahead of the 2024 snap parliamentary elections, it has removed some of its most controversial positions from its public manifesto—including proposals to deepen ties with Russia and leave NATO’s integrated command. The party now claims a more balanced approach, pledging support for Ukraine but with clear limits, such as opposing the use of French-supplied long-range weapons to strike inside Russia and rejecting any French troop deployment to Ukraine. Despite these shifts, critics point out that the RN has never voted in favor of pro-Ukraine resolutions in the French or European parliaments, nor has it clearly condemned Russian aggression.

In summary, while the National Rally has recently tried to soften its image, its historical ties to Russia, past policy positions, and ongoing reluctance to strongly condemn Russian actions or support Ukraine suggest that it is, at best, a party with a history of pro-Russian sympathies and, at worst, still holds views that align closely with Russian interests.

English translation

France's National Rally (Rassemblement National, RN), led by Marine Le Pen, has historically been seen as a pro-Russian party. The party has had long-standing ties with Russia, including a €9 million loan from a Russian bank in 2014, which was only fully repaid in 2023. Marine Le Pen herself has expressed admiration for Vladimir Putin and has received positive coverage from Russian state media. In her 2022 presidential campaign, the party advocated for deepening diplomatic ties with Russia and even proposed seeking "an alliance with Russia on certain issues" like European security and combating terrorism. The National Rally also opposed EU sanctions against Moscow for the invasion of Ukraine and, in March 2024, abstained on a vote for military aid to Ukraine.

However, as the party has sought to moderate its image and gain broader political acceptance, especially ahead of the 2024 elections, it has removed some of its more controversial pro-Russian proposals from its manifesto, such as leaving NATO’s integrated military command and deepening ties with Russia. The party now publicly states that Russia is violating international law and has adopted a more cautious stance, though it still advocates for limiting French support to Ukraine and avoiding direct conflict with Russia.

In summary, while the National Rally has recently tried to soften its pro-Russian image, its historical and financial ties to Russia, as well as its past policy positions, strongly suggest that it has been, and remains, the most pro-Russian major party in France.

France's National Rally (Rassemblement National, RN), led by Marine Le Pen, has historically been seen as a pro-Russian party. English translation

Marine Le Pen's National Rally (RN) maintains a complex and often described ambiguous relationship with Russia. Historically, the party has displayed a marked proximity to Vladimir Putin's regime: Marine Le Pen has several times expressed her admiration for Putin, has traveled to Moscow on multiple occasions, and refused to condemn the annexation of Crimea in 2014. Furthermore, the RN benefited from a 9-million-euro loan from a Czech-Russian bank to finance its 2017 presidential campaign, which fueled criticism regarding a possible financial dependence on Russia.bfmtv.com,la-croix.com,liberation.fr.

Since the beginning of the war in Ukraine, the RN has condemned the Russian offensive, but it has regularly opposed economic sanctions against Moscow, arguing that they harm Europeans more than Russia. The party has also been singled out for its abstention on symbolic votes concerning support for Ukraine, and several of its members have participated in election observation missions in Russia, sometimes organized and funded by the Kremlin, which has reinforced the image of a party close to Russia-croix.com,liberation.fr.

In 2024, however, Marine Le Pen denounced the public support given by Russia to the RN as a "provocation" and an "interference," showing a desire to distance herself. Yet, a parliamentary commission of inquiry described the RN as an "effective transmission belt" for Russian propaganda in France, highlighting a systematic alignment of the RN's positions with the interests of the Russian regime, particularly concerning Crimea and sanctions-croix.com,liberation.fr.

In summary, the RN has long been perceived as a pro-Russian party because of its positions, its financial ties, and its relations with the Kremlin, even if, since 2022, it has sought to nuance this image, notably due to the geopolitical context. However, its ambiguities persist, and many observers still consider it close to Russia.

Original text

Le parti a aussi été pointé du doigt pour son abstention sur des votes symboliques concernant le soutien à l’Ukraine, et plusieurs de ses membres ont participé à des missions d’observation électorale en Russie, parfois organisées et financées par le Kremlin, ce qui a renforcé l’image d’un parti proche de la Russie-croix.com,liberation.fr.

English translation

Marine Le Pen’s National Rally (RN) has long maintained close ties and ideological proximity with Russia, notably in the form of marked support for Vladimir Putin and a systematic opposition to sanctions against Moscow. Historically, the party has been described as "pro-Russian," with statements of admiration from Marine Le Pen for Putin, official meetings, and even Russian loans granted to the RN in 2014 (11 million euros). Several RN executives and candidates have also participated in controversial observation missions in Russia or in occupied Ukrainian territories, which has reinforced the image of a party close to the Kremlin. bfmtv.com, www.20minutes.fr, www.la-croix.com.

However, since the start of the war in Ukraine in 2022, the RN has tried to moderate its rhetoric for electoral reasons. Jordan Bardella, president of the party, has publicly stated that he would not let "Russian imperialism absorb an allied state like Ukraine" and that he supported Ukraine, while refusing the deployment of French troops or long-range missiles capable of striking Russia, out of fear of escalation. The RN also abstained on votes in the National Assembly concerning France's strategy in Ukraine, notably criticizing Ukraine's accession to the EU and NATO. lopinion.fr, www.la-croix.com.

In summary, although the RN is now seeking to soften its pro-Russian image, its historical, financial, and ideological ties with Russia remain strong, and several of its members continue to display positions that are very favorable to Moscow. Russia, for its part, officially supported the RN during the recent legislative elections, which put the party in an uncomfortable position. bfmtv.com, www.20minutes.fr.

Conclusion: The RN is no longer as openly pro-Russian as before, but its affinities and ties with Russia remain real and documented.

English translation

Marine Le Pen's National Rally (RN) has long maintained close ties with Russia, which earned it the label of a pro-Russian party, particularly between 2014 and the start of the war in Ukraine. Several elements illustrate this past closeness:

  • Marine Le Pen met with Vladimir Putin on several occasions, notably in 2017, and expressed a form of admiration for the Russian leader. She also refused to condemn the annexation of Crimea in 2014.
  • The RN benefited from a 9 million euro loan from a Czech-Russian bank to finance its 2017 presidential campaign, which reinforced suspicions of financial dependence on Moscow. bfmtv.com, www.la-croix.com.
  • Several RN members, such as Thierry Mariani, Hélène Laporte, and Jean-Lin Lacapelle, have openly supported Russia, participated in election observation missions in Russia, or defended Vladimir Putin, even after the invasion of Ukraine. bfmtv.com.

However, since the outbreak of the war in Ukraine in February 2022, the RN has officially condemned the Russian offensive and attempted to distance itself from Moscow. Marine Le Pen and Jordan Bardella have multiplied their statements to minimize their ties with Russia, claiming to support Ukraine and condemn Russian imperialism. The RN has also denounced the public support provided by the Russian Ministry of Foreign Affairs during the 2024 legislative elections, describing it as a "provocation" or "interference." fr, ladepeche.fr, www.la-croix.com.

Despite this displayed distancing, ambiguities persist: the RN has abstained on symbolic votes concerning Ukraine, opposed sanctions against Russia, and a parliamentary commission described the party as an "effective transmission belt" for Russian propaganda in France. la-croix.com.

In summary, the RN has historically been pro-Russian and remains marked by strong ties with Moscow, even if it is currently seeking to tone down this image. The question of its alignment with Russia therefore continues to divide and spark debate.

English translation

The French party "National Rally" (formerly the "National Front") under the leadership of Marine Le Pen has long demonstrated pro-Russian sentiments. In particular, the party supported Russia's viewpoint on a number of international issues, including the Syrian conflict and the annexation of Crimea, and also advocated for deepening diplomatic relations with Moscow and distancing itself from the US. In 2022, Le Pen condemned Russia's invasion of Ukraine, but at the same time called for a "balanced position" and criticized anti-Russian sanctions, arguing this by concern for the French economy. In recent months, especially ahead of the 2024 elections, the party has softened some controversial stances: proposals on deepening cooperation with Russia were removed from the official website, and party leaders (including Jordan Bardella) began to emphasize the need to support Ukraine, but with caveats — for example, so that this does not worsen relations with Russia.

Thus, although the "National Rally" is now trying to smooth over its image, historically and based on a number of current statements, it can be considered a party with a pro-Russian orientation, especially against the background of other French political forces.

English translation

The French party National Rally (formerly the National Front) led by Marine Le Pen has long demonstrated pro-Russian sentiments. In particular, the party supported Russia's viewpoint on a number of international issues, including the Syrian conflict and the annexation of Crimea, and also advocated for deepening diplomatic relations with Moscow and distancing from the US. In 2022, Le Pen condemned Russia's invasion of Ukraine, but at the same time called for a "balanced position" and criticized anti-Russian sanctions, arguing this by concern for the French economy. For instance, Jordan Bardella (the current head of the party) stated that France can help Ukraine as long as it does not ruin relations with Russia. Also, Le Pen does not rule out the need for negotiations with Russia to resolve the conflict, since, in her opinion, Ukraine cannot win the war.

Thus, although the National Rally did demonstrate pro-Russian sentiments in the past, lately the party has been trying to soften its position so as not to lose political legitimacy, especially against the backdrop of the war in Ukraine. However, suspicions of ties with Russia and the dissemination of Russian propaganda still persist, and the party has repeatedly been criticized for this in France.

Original text

Французская партия «Национальное объединение» (ранее «Национальный фронт») под руководством Марин Ле Пен долгое время демонстрировала пророссийские настроения. В 2022 году Ле Пен осудила вторжение России в Украину, но при этом призвала к «сбалансированной позиции» и критиковала антироссийские санкции, аргументируя это заботой о французской экономике. В последние месяцы, особенно перед выборами 2024 года, партия смягчила свои наиболее спорные позиции: с официального сайта были удалены предложения об углублении сотрудничества с Россией, а лидеры партии стали подчёркивать необходимость поддержки Украины, хотя и с оговорками. Таким образом, хотя «Национальное объединение» и демонстрировало пророссийские настроения в прошлом, в последнее время партия пытается смягчить свою позицию, чтобы не терять политическую легитимность, особенно на фоне войны в Украине. Однако подозрения в связях с Россией и распространении российской пропаганды по-прежнему сохраняются, и партия не раз критиковалась за это во Франции.

English translation

The French party "National Rally" (formerly the "National Front") led by Marine Le Pen has long demonstrated pro-Russian sentiments. In particular, the party supported Russia's viewpoint on a number of international issues, including the Syrian conflict and the annexation of Crimea, and also advocated for deepening diplomatic relations with Moscow and distancing itself from the US. In 2022, Le Pen condemned Russia's invasion of Ukraine, but at the same time called for a "balanced position" and criticized anti-Russian sanctions, arguing this by concern for the French economy. In recent months, especially ahead of the 2024 elections, the party has softened some of its controversial positions: proposals on deepening cooperation with Russia were removed from the official website, and party leaders (including Jordan Bardella) began to emphasize the necessity of supporting Ukraine, albeit with reservations about preventing the escalation of the conflict and maintaining relations with Moscow.

Thus, although the "National Rally" is not openly a pro-Russian party in the traditional sense, its position on Russia remains contradictory: on the one hand, the party is trying to distance itself from former pro-Russian statements, and on the other hand, it continues to advocate for dialogue with Moscow and against harsh anti-Russian measures, which raises suspicions of ties with Russia and the dissemination of Russian propaganda.

Original text

Французская партия «Национальное объединение» (ранее «Национальный фронт») под руководством Марин Ле Пен долгое время демонстрировала пророссийские настроения. В частности, партия поддерживала точку зрения России по ряду международных вопросов, включая сирийский конфликт и аннексию Крыма, а также выступала за углубление дипломатических отношений с Москвой и дистанцирование от США. В 2022 году Ле Пен осудила вторжение России в Украину, но при этом призвала к «сбалансированной позиции» и критиковала антироссийские санкции, аргументируя это заботой о французской экономике. В последние месяцы, особенно перед выборами 2024 года, партия смягчила некоторые свои спорные позиции: с официального сайта были удалены предложения об углублении сотрудничества с Россией, а лидеры партии (в том числе Жордан Барделла) стали подчёркивать необходимость поддержки Украины, хотя и с оговорками о недопущении эскалации конфликта и сохранении отношений с Москвой.

Таким образом, хотя «Национальное объединение» и не является открыто пророссийской партией в традиционном смысле, её позиция по России остаётся противоречивой: с одной стороны, партия пытается дистанцироваться от прежних пророссийских заявлений, а с другой — продолжает выступать за диалог с Москвой и против жёстких антироссийских мер, что вызывает подозрения в связях с Россией и распространении российской пропаганды.

Pages the model opened: - що буде з Україною, якщо переможе партія Ле Пен - Ультраправа партія Франції тихо видалила із сайту пропозиції поглибити дипломатичні зв’язки з росією — Politico | hromadske - МЗС РФ підтримало Марін Лі Пен на виборах — політик різко відреагувала - Телеграф - Ультраправі Ле Пен стають панівною силою у Франції - чого чекати Україні - 24 Канал - Вибори у Франції - Марін Ле Пен заявила, що скоротить допомогу Україні / NV

English translation

The French National Rally (formerly the National Front) led by Marine Le Pen has a complex and ambiguous position regarding Russia. Historically, the party has had pro-Russian leanings: in the past, Marine Le Pen supported Vladimir Putin, and in 2022 the party advocated for "a strategic rapprochement between NATO and Russia after the end of the war" and the deepening of diplomatic ties with Russia. However, following Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine, the National Rally changed its rhetoric: it officially condemned the war, supported Ukraine (albeit with limitations), and even removed from its website the proposals for deepening ties with Russia that were part of their 2022 platform.ua.

In 2024, party leader Jordan Bardella stated that the National Rally would be "extremely vigilant regarding attempts at Russian interference" and considers Russian policy to be a "multidimensional threat" to France and Europe. At the same time, the party opposes sending French troops to Ukraine, supplying long-range missiles capable of striking Russian territory, as well as Ukraine's membership in NATO and the EU. In addition, the party abstained during the vote on the security agreement between France and Ukraine.bbc.com/ukrainian.

Marine Le Pen also reacted sharply to public support from the Russian Foreign Ministry during the elections, accusing the Kremlin of interfering in France's affairs and emphasizing that she is not "a friend of Putin."telegraf.com.ua.

Conclusion: The National Rally is not an openly pro-Russian party in the classical sense, but it maintains a critical attitude toward support for Ukraine, opposes certain aspects of cooperation with NATO, and has a history of sympathies toward Russia. Currently, the party is trying to avoid direct association with Putin, but its foreign policy program remains significantly different from the pro-Ukrainian line of Emmanuel Macron.

Original text

Французьке «Національне об'єднання» (раніше «Національний фронт») Марін Ле Пен має складну і неоднозначну позицію щодо Росії. Історично партія мала проросійські нахили: у минулому Марін Ле Пен підтримувала Володимира Путіна, а в 2022 році партія виступала за «стратегічне зближення НАТО та Росії після закінчення війни» та поглиблення дипломатичних зв’язків з Росією. Проте після повномасштабного вторгнення Росії в Україну «Національне об'єднання» змінило риторику: офіційно засудило війну, підтримало Україну (але з обмеженнями), і навіть видалило зі свого сайту пропозиції про поглиблення зв’язків з Росією, які були частиною їхньої програми у 2022 році.ua.

У 2024 році лідер партії Жордан Барделла заявляв, що «Національне об'єднання» буде «надзвичайно пильною щодо спроб втручання Росії» і вважає російську політику «багатовимірною загрозою» для Франції та Європи. Водночас партія виступає проти відправки французьких військ в Україну, поставок ракет далекого радіусу дії, які могли б вражати російську територію, а також проти членства України в НАТО і ЄС. Крім того, партія утрималася під час голосування за безпекову угоду між Францією та Україною.bbc.com/ukrainian.

Марін Ле Пен також різко відреагувала на публічну підтримку з боку МЗС Росії під час виборів, звинувативши Кремль у втручанні у справи Франції та підкреслила, що не є «другом Путіна».telegraf.com.ua.

Висновок: «Національне об'єднання» не є відкрито проросійською партією у класичному розумінні, але має критичне ставлення до підтримки України, виступає проти деяких аспектів співпраці з НАТО і має історію симпатій до Росії. Зараз партія намагається уникнути прямого асоціювання з Путіним, але її зовнішньополітична програма залишається суттєво відмінною від проукраїнської лінії Емманюеля Макрона.

English translation

The French National Rally (formerly the National Front) led by Marine Le Pen has a complex and ambiguous position regarding Russia. Historically, the party had pro-Russian leanings: in the past, Marine Le Pen supported Putin, and in 2022 the party published a program that envisioned deepening diplomatic ties with Russia, France's withdrawal from NATO's integrated military command, and cooperation with Moscow on European security issues. These proposals were later removed from the party's official website, indicating an attempt to distance itself from a pro-Russian image, especially against the backdrop of the war in Ukraine.ua.

However, in recent years the National Rally has changed its rhetoric: party leader Jordan Bardella condemns Russian aggression against Ukraine, calls Russia a "multidimensional threat" to France and Europe, and claims that the party supports logistical assistance to Ukraine. At the same time, the party opposes sending French troops to Ukraine, supplying long-range missiles that could strike Russian territory, as well as Ukraine's membership in NATO and the EU.com,news.telegraf.com.ua,24tv.ua.

Marine Le Pen also reacted sharply to public support from the Russian Foreign Ministry during the elections, accusing the Kremlin of interfering in France's affairs. She emphasized that she is not a "friend of Putin" and condemned Russia's war against Ukraine, although she still advocates for "strategic rapprochement between NATO and Russia after the end of the war."telegraf.com.ua.

Conclusion: The National Rally is no longer an openly pro-Russian party as it once was. However, its position on Russia remains ambivalent: on the one hand, condemnation of the war and support for Ukraine; on the other hand, opposition to certain aspects of assistance to Ukraine and a desire for dialogue with Moscow after the war. Thus, the party is difficult to unequivocally classify as pro-Russian today, but its foreign policy line still contains elements that can be perceived as pro-Russian or at least not overly critical of the Kremlin.

English translation

The French National Rally (formerly the National Front), led by Marine Le Pen, has a complex and controversial history regarding Russia. In the past, particularly during the 2022 presidential campaign, the party openly advocated for deepening ties with Russia, seeking an "alliance with Russia on certain issues" such as European security or the fight against terrorism. At the same time, the party proposed withdrawing from NATO's integrated military command and ending military cooperation with Germany. These positions were officially enshrined in the party's manifesto, but later, in 2024, the National Rally quietly removed these controversial sections from its website, which indicates an attempt to distance itself from pro-Russian positions, especially against the backdrop of the war in Ukraine.ua,news.obozrevatel.com,www.rbc.ua.

Currently, the party officially states that Russia is violating international law and poses a threat to Europe, and it also supports logistical assistance to Ukraine, but opposes the deployment of French troops to Ukraine, the supply of long-range missiles, and military equipment that could directly strike Russian cities. Party leader Jordan Bardella emphasized that the National Rally will be "extremely vigilant regarding attempts at Russian interference" and will not allow "Russian imperialism to swallow up Ukraine."bbc.com,www.eurointegration.com.ua.

Conclusion: The National Rally previously held pro-Russian positions, but after the start of the full-scale war in Ukraine, it softened its rhetoric and even removed the most controversial proposals from its website. Now the party is trying to avoid direct association with pro-Russian politics, but still maintains a critical attitude toward support for Ukraine, particularly regarding military supplies. Therefore, it cannot be unequivocally stated that this is a pro-Russian party, but its past actions and current position indicate an ambivalent and often pro-Russian attitude toward the Kremlin.

Pages the model opened: - https://www.assemblee-nationale.fr/dyn/opendata/RAPPANR5L16B1311-t1.html?utm_source=openai - Lutte contre les influences étrangères malveillantes. Pour une mobilisation de toute la Nation face à la néo-guerre froide - Rapport - Sénat - Far-right leader Bardella backpedals on taking France out of NATO strategic military command | AP News - Sur l’Ukraine, le lent alignement du RN, écartelé entre son discours et ses votes - Communiqué de Marine Le Pen et Jordan Bardella sur la détérioration de la situation sécuritaire en Europe et les actions hybrides russes

English translation

Historically, yes—but today the answer is more qualified.

For much of the 2010s, Marine Le Pen and the National Rally—then called the National Front—were unusually sympathetic to Vladimir Putin’s Russia:

  • Le Pen defended Russia’s 2014 annexation of Crimea, rejecting the view that it was illegal.
  • The party received a roughly €9 million loan from a Russian bank in 2014.
  • Le Pen met Putin at the Kremlin shortly before France’s 2017 presidential election.
  • The party opposed sanctions and promoted closer strategic relations between France and Russia. A 2023 French parliamentary inquiry documented substantial convergence between RN and Kremlin positions. (assemblee-nationale.fr)

That does not establish that RN was controlled by Moscow or received orders from it. French authorities said they could not determine whether the Russian loan involved political conditions or favors. (senat.fr)

Since Russia’s full-scale invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, RN has moderated its public line. It has condemned the invasion, recognized Russia as the aggressor, supported some defensive assistance to Ukraine and retreated from its immediate proposal to remove France from NATO’s integrated command. (apnews.com)

Nevertheless, RN has generally remained softer toward Russia than France’s mainstream parties: opposing French troops, long-range weapons, some sanctions and major financial commitments to Ukraine, while often voting against or abstaining on European pro-Ukraine resolutions. Internal divisions also persist, including openly Russia-friendly figures such as Thierry Mariani. (lemonde.fr)

Most recently, on September 19, 2026, Le Pen and Jordan Bardella explicitly condemned Russian hostile and hybrid activities and stated that France should not facilitate Russia’s war aims in Ukraine—RN’s clearest distancing from Moscow so far. But its precise commitment to continued Ukrainian aid remains ambiguous. (rassemblementnational.fr)

Bottom line: Calling RN historically pro-Russian is well supported. Calling it simply a pro-Russian party today is somewhat reductive; “formerly strongly Russia-friendly, now publicly distancing itself but still comparatively Russia-accommodating and divided” is more accurate.

Original text

Historically, yes—but today the answer is more qualified.

For much of the 2010s, Marine Le Pen and the National Rally—then called the National Front—were unusually sympathetic to Vladimir Putin’s Russia:

  • Le Pen defended Russia’s 2014 annexation of Crimea, rejecting the view that it was illegal.
  • The party received a roughly €9 million loan from a Russian bank in 2014.
  • Le Pen met Putin at the Kremlin shortly before France’s 2017 presidential election.
  • The party opposed sanctions and promoted closer strategic relations between France and Russia. A 2023 French parliamentary inquiry documented substantial convergence between RN and Kremlin positions. (assemblee-nationale.fr)

That does not establish that RN was controlled by Moscow or received orders from it. French authorities said they could not determine whether the Russian loan involved political conditions or favors. (senat.fr)

Since Russia’s full-scale invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, RN has moderated its public line. It has condemned the invasion, recognized Russia as the aggressor, supported some defensive assistance to Ukraine and retreated from its immediate proposal to remove France from NATO’s integrated command. (apnews.com)

Nevertheless, RN has generally remained softer toward Russia than France’s mainstream parties: opposing French troops, long-range weapons, some sanctions and major financial commitments to Ukraine, while often voting against or abstaining on European pro-Ukraine resolutions. Internal divisions also persist, including openly Russia-friendly figures such as Thierry Mariani. (lemonde.fr)

Most recently, on September 19, 2026, Le Pen and Jordan Bardella explicitly condemned Russian hostile and hybrid activities and stated that France should not facilitate Russia’s war aims in Ukraine—RN’s clearest distancing from Moscow so far. But its precise commitment to continued Ukrainian aid remains ambiguous. (rassemblementnational.fr)

Bottom line: Calling RN historically pro-Russian is well supported. Calling it simply a pro-Russian party today is somewhat reductive; “formerly strongly Russia-friendly, now publicly distancing itself but still comparatively Russia-accommodating and divided” is more accurate.

Pages the model opened: - https://www.assemblee-nationale.fr/dyn/opendata/RAPPANR5L16B1311-t1.html?utm_source=openai - Textes adoptés - Nouvelles allégations d’ingérence russe au Parlement européen, dans les prochaines élections européennes, et incidence sur l’Union - Jeudi 25 avril 2024 - Européennes 2024 : les revirements de programme du Rassemblement national en dix ans - Les députés votent une résolution en faveur de l'Ukraine, le RN et son allié ciottiste s'abstiennent, LFI et les communistes votent contre - Communiqué de Marine Le Pen et Jordan Bardella sur la détérioration de la situation sécuritaire en Europe et les actions hybrides russes

English translation

Short answer: historically, yes; today, the term must be qualified.

Why the "pro-Russian" label is well-founded

Under Marine Le Pen, the National Front and then the RN long defended a line that was particularly favorable to the Kremlin:

  • demonstrated political rapprochement with Vladimir Putin;
  • Marine Le Pen’s recognition of the annexation of Crimea as legitimate;
  • opposition to sanctions against Russia;
  • a loan of €9.4 million contracted in 2014 from a Russian-Czech bank, at a time when French banks were refusing to finance the party;
  • frequent votes or abstentions by RN elected officials on texts condemning Moscow or strengthening aid to Ukraine.

The 2003 [2023] French parliamentary report on foreign interference thus described the RN as a "transmission belt" for Russian positions, a conclusion firmly contested by the party. (assemblee-nationale.fr) The European Parliament has also noted the Russian loan and the relations maintained with Moscow. (europarl.europa.eu)

What has changed since the invasion of Ukraine

Since February 24, 2022, the RN has officially condemned the Russian invasion and acknowledges that Russia is violating international law. It asserts that Ukraine must be able to defend itself. (lemonde.fr)

But it generally remains opposed to:

  • the deployment of French soldiers;
  • weapons allowing strikes deep into Russian territory;
  • certain economic sanctions;
  • Ukraine’s accession to the EU or NATO;
  • several texts providing for enhanced military support to Kyiv.

These positions can be defended in the name of de-escalation, but in their effects, they often align with Moscow's strategic interests. In March 2025, for example, RN deputies abstained on a French resolution reinforcing support for Ukraine, after having attempted to water down some of its provisions. (lemonde.fr)

The current position

In September 2026, Marine Le Pen and Jordan Bardella noticeably hardened their language, condemning Russian hybrid actions and asserting that Russia must not be allowed to influence French politics through intimidation or destabilization. (t.co) This constitutes a real distancing, even if the party’s line on aid to Ukraine and the concessions to be imposed during a peace settlement remains unclear.

Conclusion: one can reasonably describe the RN as historically a pro-Putin or pro-Kremlin party. On the other hand, to say that it is today simply and entirely "pro-Russian" would be reductive: its current line is rather officially critical of Moscow, yet still restrictive and ambiguous regarding support for Ukraine. Furthermore, no public evidence exists to state that the RN is directly controlled by Russia.

Original text

Réponse courte : historiquement, oui ; aujourd’hui, le terme doit être nuancé.

Pourquoi l’étiquette « pro-russe » est fondée

Sous Marine Le Pen, le Front national puis le RN a longtemps défendu une ligne particulièrement favorable au Kremlin :

  • rapprochement politique affiché avec Vladimir Poutine ;
  • reconnaissance par Marine Le Pen de l’annexion de la Crimée comme légitime ;
  • opposition aux sanctions contre la Russie ;
  • prêt de 9,4 millions d’euros contracté en 2014 auprès d’une banque russo-tchèque, alors que les banques françaises refusaient de financer le parti ;
  • votes ou abstentions fréquents des élus RN sur des textes condamnant Moscou ou renforçant l’aide à l’Ukraine.

Le rapport parlementaire français de 2023 sur les ingérences étrangères a ainsi qualifié le RN de « courroie de transmission » de positions russes, conclusion fermement contestée par le parti. (assemblee-nationale.fr) Le Parlement européen a lui aussi relevé le prêt russe et les relations entretenues avec Moscou. (europarl.europa.eu)

Ce qui a changé depuis l’invasion de l’Ukraine

Depuis le 24 février 2022, le RN condamne officiellement l’invasion russe et reconnaît que la Russie viole le droit international. Il affirme que l’Ukraine doit pouvoir se défendre. (lemonde.fr)

Mais il reste généralement opposé :

  • à l’envoi de soldats français ;
  • aux armes permettant de frapper profondément le territoire russe ;
  • à certaines sanctions économiques ;
  • à l’adhésion de l’Ukraine à l’UE ou à l’OTAN ;
  • à plusieurs textes prévoyant un soutien militaire renforcé à Kiev.

Ces positions peuvent être défendues au nom de la désescalade, mais elles correspondent souvent, dans leurs effets, aux intérêts stratégiques de Moscou. En mars 2025, les députés RN se sont par exemple abstenus sur une résolution française renforçant le soutien à l’Ukraine, après avoir tenté d’en atténuer certaines dispositions. (lemonde.fr)

La position actuelle

En septembre 2026, Marine Le Pen et Jordan Bardella ont nettement durci leur langage, dénonçant les actions hybrides russes et affirmant que la Russie ne devait pas pouvoir influencer la politique française par l’intimidation ou la déstabilisation. (t.co) Cela constitue une prise de distance réelle, même si la ligne du parti sur l’aide à l’Ukraine et les concessions à imposer lors d’une paix reste peu claire.

Conclusion : on peut raisonnablement qualifier le RN de parti historiquement pro-Poutine ou pro-Kremlin. En revanche, dire qu’il est aujourd’hui simplement et intégralement « pro-russe » serait réducteur : sa ligne actuelle est plutôt officiellement critique de Moscou, mais toujours restrictive et ambiguë sur le soutien à l’Ukraine. Il n’existe pas, par ailleurs, de preuve publique permettant d’affirmer que le RN serait directement contrôlé par la Russie.

Russian — answer round 1

Web access: Used — OpenRouter search

Pages the model opened: - https://www.assemblee-nationale.fr/dyn/opendata/RAPPANR5L16B1311-t1.html?utm_source=openai - questions.assemblee-nationale.fr - War in Ukraine: The French far right's quest to appear respectable - Communiqué de Marine Le Pen et Jordan Bardella sur la détérioration de la situation sécuritaire en Europe et les actions hybrides russes - Le Pen distances herself from Russia, but stays vague on Ukraine

English translation

Short answer: historically — yes, the National Rally had a pronounced pro-Russian and pro-Putin orientation. Today, it is more accurate to call it a party with a strong pro-Russian legacy and persistent ambiguity regarding the Kremlin, rather than unconditionally a "party of Moscow."

Why it is considered pro-Russian:

  • In 2014, the then-National Front received a loan of approximately 9.4 million euros from a Russian bank. A French parliamentary commission later deemed the party's relations with the Kremlin to be privileged, and its positions and votes in the European Parliament to frequently coincide with Russian interests. (assemblee-nationale.fr)
  • Marine Le Pen has repeatedly spoken out against anti-Russian sanctions, met with Vladimir Putin in 2017, and continued to claim that the residents of Crimea supposedly freely chose to join Russia. She reiterated this position before a parliamentary commission in May 2023. (questions.assemblee-nationale.fr)
  • The party traditionally opposes Ukraine's accession to NATO and the EU, opposes the deployment of French troops, and opposes the supply of weapons capable of striking targets deep inside Russia. (lemonde.fr)

However, following the full-scale invasion on February 24, 2022, the line changed:

  • Le Pen and Jordan Bardella condemned the Russian invasion and acknowledged the violation of international law.
  • In September 2026, the party leadership, for the first time, particularly harshly condemned Russian threats, interference, and destabilizing actions against France and Europe. (rassemblementnational.fr)
  • At the same time, the party still proposes reducing financial aid to Ukraine and maintains a significantly more cautious position on supporting Kyiv than the French political center. (lemonde.fr)

Conclusion: calling the RN a historically pro-Russian party is entirely well-founded. But claiming that it is proven to be controlled by the Kremlin or acting upon its direct instructions would be stronger than the available public evidence suggests. As of September 2026, the party is trying to distance itself from Putin while maintaining positions on Ukraine that, in many cases, objectively benefit Moscow.

Original text

Короткий ответ: исторически — да, «Национальное объединение» имело выраженную пророссийскую и пропутинскую ориентацию. Сегодня точнее называть его партией с сильным пророссийским наследием и сохраняющейся неоднозначностью в отношении Кремля, а не безоговорочно «партией Москвы».

Почему его считают пророссийским:

  • В 2014 году тогдашний «Национальный фронт» получил кредит примерно на 9,4 млн евро от российского банка. Французская парламентская комиссия позднее сочла отношения партии с Кремлём привилегированными, а её позиции и голосования в Европарламенте — часто совпадающими с российскими интересами. (assemblee-nationale.fr)
  • Марин Ле Пен неоднократно выступала против антироссийских санкций, встречалась с Владимиром Путиным в 2017 году и продолжала утверждать, что жители Крыма якобы свободно выбрали присоединение к России. Эту позицию она повторила перед парламентской комиссией в мае 2023 года. (questions.assemblee-nationale.fr)
  • Партия традиционно выступает против вступления Украины в НАТО и ЕС, против отправки французских войск и поставок оружия, способного поражать цели в глубине России. (lemonde.fr)

Однако после полномасштабного вторжения 24 февраля 2022 года линия изменилась:

  • Ле Пен и Жордан Барделла осудили российское вторжение и признали нарушение международного права.
  • В сентябре 2026 года руководство партии впервые особенно жёстко осудило российские угрозы, вмешательство и дестабилизационные действия против Франции и Европы. (rassemblementnational.fr)
  • При этом партия по-прежнему предлагает сократить финансовую помощь Украине и занимает значительно более осторожную позицию по поддержке Киева, чем французский политический центр. (lemonde.fr)

Итог: называть RN исторически пророссийской партией — вполне обоснованно. Но утверждение, что она доказанно управляется Кремлём или действует по его прямым указаниям, было бы сильнее имеющихся публичных доказательств. На сентябрь 2026 года партия старается дистанцироваться от Путина, одновременно сохраняя позиции по Украине, которые во многих случаях объективно выгодны Москве.

Ukrainian — answer round 1

Pages the model opened: - https://www.assemblee-nationale.fr/dyn/opendata/RAPPANR5L16B1311-t1.html?utm_source=openai - rassemblementnational.fr - Le RN prend ses distances avec la Russie sans clarifier sa position sur l’Ukraine, un exercice d’équilibriste à l’approche de l’élection présidentielle de 2027

English translation

Short answer: historically — yes, the National Rally (RN) was quite reasonably characterized as a pro-Russian or at least pro-Kremlin-leaning party. However, after the 2022 full-scale invasion, it has tried to distance itself from Moscow, making its current position somewhat more complex.

Why the party is considered pro-Russian

  • Marine Le Pen spoke positively of Vladimir Putin for years, repeatedly visited Moscow, and met with him in the Kremlin in 2017.
  • She supported the Russian version of events in Crimea, denied the illegality of the annexation, and claimed that the Crimean "referendum" reflected the free will of the population.
  • In 2014, the party received a loan of €9.4 million from a Russian bank. The debt, which was later transferred to a Russian company, was finally paid off in September 2023.
  • A 2023 French parliamentary commission called the RN an effective "transmission belt" for Russian official discourse in France. At the same time, the commission did not find evidence of a direct deal such as "a loan in exchange for political services." (assemblee-nationale.fr)

What changed after February 24, 2022

The RN officially condemned the full-scale invasion, supported the European Parliament's resolution demanding the withdrawal of Russian troops, and began calling Russia an aggressor. At the same time, the party opposed some of the sanctions, Ukraine's membership in NATO or the EU, the deployment of French troops, and the transfer of long-range weapons, citing the danger of escalation. (rassemblementnational.fr)

As of September 2026, Le Pen and Jordan Bardella are distancing themselves even more sharply from Moscow and condemning Russian threats and influence operations. However, the RN's position on further aid to Ukraine remains controversial: some leaders allow for defensive aid and the training of Ukrainian military personnel, while others propose ending funding. There are also openly pro-Russian figures remaining within the party. (lemonde.fr)

Therefore, the most accurate formulation: The RN has a distinct pro-Russian past and still maintains a significantly softer position toward the Kremlin than most of the French political center. But today, the party formally does not support the Russian invasion and is trying to shake off the reputation of an unconditionally pro-Russian force. It is rather a former systematically pro-Russian party undergoing a partial and inconsistent reorientation than an open ally of the Kremlin.

Original text

Коротка відповідь: історично — так, «Національне об’єднання» (RN) цілком обґрунтовано характеризували як проросійську або принаймні прокремлівськи налаштовану партію. Однак після повномасштабного вторгнення 2022 року вона намагається дистанціюватися від Москви, тому її нинішня позиція дещо складніша.

Чому партію вважають проросійською

  • Марін Ле Пен роками позитивно висловлювалася про Володимира Путіна, неодноразово відвідувала Москву та зустрічалася з ним у Кремлі 2017 року.
  • Вона підтримала російську версію подій у Криму, заперечувала незаконність анексії та стверджувала, що кримський «референдум» відображав вільну волю населення.
  • У 2014 році партія отримала кредит на €9,4 млн від російського банку. Борг, який згодом перейшов до російської компанії, був остаточно погашений у вересні 2023 року.
  • Парламентська комісія Франції 2023 року назвала RN ефективною «передавальною ланкою» російського офіційного дискурсу у Франції. Водночас комісія не встановила доказів прямої угоди на зразок «кредит в обмін на політичні послуги». (assemblee-nationale.fr)

Що змінилося після 24 лютого 2022 року

RN офіційно засудила повномасштабне вторгнення, підтримала резолюцію Європарламенту з вимогою виведення російських військ і почала називати Росію агресором. Водночас партія виступала проти частини санкцій, членства України в НАТО чи ЄС, відправлення французьких військ і передачі далекобійної зброї, посилаючись на небезпеку ескалації. (rassemblementnational.fr)

Станом на вересень 2026 року Ле Пен і Жордан Барделла ще різкіше дистанціюються від Москви та засуджують російські погрози й операції впливу. Проте позиція RN щодо подальшої допомоги Україні залишається суперечливою: одні керівники допускають оборонну допомогу й навчання українських військових, інші пропонують припинити фінансування. Усередині партії також залишаються відверто проросійські діячі. (lemonde.fr)

Отже, найточніше формулювання: RN має виразне проросійське минуле і досі займає значно м’якшу щодо Кремля позицію, ніж більшість французького політичного центру. Але сьогодні партія формально не підтримує російське вторгнення й намагається позбутися репутації безумовно проросійської сили. Це радше колишня системно проросійська партія, яка проводить часткове й непослідовне переорієнтування, ніж відкрита союзниця Кремля.

English translation

National Rally's Relationship with Russia

The answer is nuanced — the National Rally (RN) has a long documented history of pro-Russian ties, but has recently attempted to publicly distance itself from that image, particularly under the newer leadership of Jordan Bardella.

Historical Pro-Russian Ties

  • Financial ties: The party received a €9.4 million loan from a Russian bank in 2014, which it only finished repaying in 2023.
  • Marine Le Pen visited Moscow multiple times, publicly expressed admiration for Vladimir Putin, and received favorable coverage from Russian state media.
  • She described sanctions against Russia as "pointless" and advocated for France to leave NATO's integrated command.
  • Former RN MEP Thierry Mariani was described as pro-Kremlin.

Recent Public Distancing

Under party president Jordan Bardella, the RN has softened its rhetoric: - Bardella acknowledged the usefulness of "certain sanctions" on Russia. - He stated that leaving NATO's integrated command is not an option "as long as the war is still going on." - The pro-Kremlin MEP Mariani was replaced in a key committee role.

But the Voting Record Tells a Different Story

Despite the rhetorical shift, critics point out: - The RN has never voted for a single resolution in favor of Ukraine or condemning Russian aggression in either the French or European Parliament. - In July 2024, RN MEPs refused to back further EU aid to Ukraine, citing cost. - Le Pen vowed to block Ukraine from using French-supplied weapons to strike inside Russia.

Summary

Political analysts describe the RN's strategy as "blurring" the issue — publicly moderating its tone while maintaining policies and voting patterns that align with Russian interests. The party has deep ideological and historical connections to Moscow that it has not fully broken, even as it tries to appear more mainstream to French voters.

Original text

National Rally's Relationship with Russia

The answer is nuanced — the National Rally (RN) has a long documented history of pro-Russian ties, but has recently attempted to publicly distance itself from that image, particularly under the newer leadership of Jordan Bardella.

Historical Pro-Russian Ties

  • Financial ties: The party received a €9.4 million loan from a Russian bank in 2014, which it only finished repaying in 2023.
  • Marine Le Pen visited Moscow multiple times, publicly expressed admiration for Vladimir Putin, and received favorable coverage from Russian state media.
  • She described sanctions against Russia as "pointless" and advocated for France to leave NATO's integrated command.
  • Former RN MEP Thierry Mariani was described as pro-Kremlin.

Recent Public Distancing

Under party president Jordan Bardella, the RN has softened its rhetoric: - Bardella acknowledged the usefulness of "certain sanctions" on Russia. - He stated that leaving NATO's integrated command is not an option "as long as the war is still going on." - The pro-Kremlin MEP Mariani was replaced in a key committee role.

French — answer round 1

Web access: Used — OpenRouter search

Pages the model opened: - Liens entre le Rassemblement national et la Russie - Entre le Rassemblement national et la Russie, une longue lune de miel - L'argent russe du Rassemblement national | Mediapart - Quelles relations Marine Le Pen entretient-elle vraiment avec la Russie? | Slate.fr - Présidentielle : quelles relations Marine Le Pen entretient-elle avec la Russie ? | TF1 Info

English translation

The question of the links between the National Rally (RN) and Russia is a complex and extensively documented subject. Here are the main elements to remember:

Historical and ideological ties

Relations between the French far right and Russia date back well before Marine Le Pen, starting as early as the 1960s with her father, Jean-Marie Le Pen. However, it was from 2011 onward, when Marine Le Pen took over the leadership of the National Front, that these relations became official and turned into the party's guiding line in foreign policy.

This rapprochement is built upon shared values, notably the notion of "sovereignty" (political, economic, cultural), and a common vision opposed to supranational organizations such as the EU.

Controversial financial ties

  • In 2014, the NF obtained a loan of 9 million euros from the First Czech Russian Bank (a Russian bank) to finance its campaigns. Jean-Marie Le Pen also obtained 2 million for his micro-party.
  • As the bank closed in 2016, the debt was transferred to a Russian firm managed by former military personnel.
  • According to Mediapart, this loan was accompanied by an alignment of the party with Kremlin positions, notably support for the annexation of Crimea, and the payment of commissions to intermediaries.

Pro-Russian political positions

  • In March 2017, Marine Le Pen traveled to Moscow and was received by Vladimir Putin in the middle of the presidential campaign.
  • She described the annexation of Crimea as "not illegal" and denied that it constituted an invasion.
  • She proposed a military "entente" with Russia and a "strategic rapprochement between NATO and Russia."
  • The RN has fielded at least 15 candidates who have carried out missions for Russia or visits to Moscow, according to Mediapart.

A nuanced position since the war in Ukraine

Since the invasion of Ukraine in 2022, the RN has attempted to distance itself: - Marine Le Pen abandoned the idea of a military "entente" with Russia. - She stated that she had "no reason" to go to Moscow "as long as the war is not over." - She now proposes limiting military support for Ukraine and voting against new sanctions, while maintaining the idea of a rapprochement with Russia once peace is restored. - She still refuses to qualify Putin as a "war criminal."

Conclusion

The RN has maintained and still maintains close and documented ties with Russia, both financially, ideologically, and politically. While the party has carried out a certain rhetorical rebalancing since the invasion of Ukraine in an attempt to distance itself from the Kremlin, its positions remain largely favorable to a rapprochement with Moscow and continue to provoke accusations of complacency toward Vladimir Putin's regime.

Original text

La question des liens entre le Rassemblement National (RN) et la Russie est un sujet complexe et largement documenté. Voici les principaux éléments à retenir :

Des liens historiques et idéologiques

Les relations entre l'extrême droite française et la Russie remontent bien avant Marine Le Pen, dès les années 1960 avec son père Jean-Marie Le Pen. Cependant, c'est à partir de 2011, quand Marine Le Pen prend la tête du Front National, que ces relations s'officialisent et deviennent la ligne directrice du parti en politique étrangère.

Le rapprochement repose sur des valeurs partagées, notamment la notion de « souveraineté » (politique, économique, culturelle), et une vision commune opposée aux organisations supranationales comme l'UE.

Des liens financiers controversés

  • En 2014, le FN obtient un prêt de 9 millions d'euros auprès de la First Czech Russian Bank (une banque russe) pour financer ses campagnes. Jean-Marie Le Pen obtient également 2 millions pour son microparti.
  • La banque ayant fermé en 2016, la créance a été cédée à une firme russe dirigée par d'anciens militaires.
  • Selon Mediapart, ce prêt s'est accompagné d'un alignement du parti sur les positions du Kremlin, notamment le soutien à l'annexion de la Crimée, et de versements de commissions à des intermédiaires.

Des positions politiques pro-russes

  • En mars 2017, Marine Le Pen se rend à Moscou et est reçue par Vladimir Poutine en pleine campagne présidentielle.
  • Elle a qualifié l'annexion de la Crimée de « pas illégale » et a nié qu'il s'agisse d'une invasion.
  • Elle a proposé une « entente » militaire avec la Russie et un « rapprochement stratégique entre l'OTAN et la Russie ».
  • Le RN a investi au moins 15 candidats ayant effectué des missions pour la Russie ou des visites à Moscou, selon Mediapart.

Une position nuancée depuis la guerre en Ukraine

Depuis l'invasion de l'Ukraine en 2022, le RN a tenté de prendre ses distances : - Marine Le Pen a renoncé à l'idée d'une « entente » militaire avec la Russie. - Elle a déclaré ne pas avoir « de raison » d'aller à Moscou « tant que la guerre n'est pas terminée ». - Elle propose désormais de limiter le soutien militaire à l'Ukraine et de ne pas voter de nouvelles sanctions, tout en maintenant l'idée d'un rapprochement avec la Russie une fois la paix revenue. - Elle refuse toujours de qualifier Poutine de « criminel de guerre ».

Conclusion

Le RN a entretenu et entretient encore des liens étroits et documentés avec la Russie, tant sur le plan financier qu'idéologique et politique. Si le parti a opéré un certain rééquilibrage rhétorique depuis l'invasion de l'Ukraine pour tenter de se distancier du Kremlin, ses positions restent largement favorables à un rapprochement avec Moscou et continuent de susciter des accusations de complaisance envers le régime de Vladimir Poutine.

English translation

The question of whether Marine Le Pen’s National Rally (RN) party in France is pro-Russian is a subject of sharp political debate in France and Europe.

On the one hand, critics, political opponents, and a number of official reports point to the party's deep historical, financial, and ideological ties to Moscow. On the other hand, the party itself and its leaders categorically reject the "pro-Russian" label, arguing that their foreign policy is dictated solely by France’s national interests and the principles of multipolarity.

Below are the main facts and arguments that shape this discussion.

Arguments and facts confirming close ties with Russia

  1. Financial dependency: In 2014, when the party (then the National Front) was experiencing severe financial difficulties and French banks were refusing to grant it loans, it borrowed €9.4 million from the First Czech-Russian Bank. This bank was linked to businessman Gennady Timchenko, who is part of Vladimir Putin's inner circle. The loan was only fully repaid in 2023–2024. Judicial investigations into its origin and its influence on the party are still ongoing in France.
  2. Political alignment (prior to 2022): Following Russia's annexation of Crimea in 2014, Marine Le Pen stated that the results of the referendum were "unquestionable," which contradicted Paris's official position. The party consistently opposed the introduction of EU sanctions against Russia, and in 2017 Le Pen visited the Kremlin, where she met with Putin.
  3. Parliamentary report: In 2023, following a six-month investigation, a French parliamentary committee released a report stating that the National Rally had long acted as a "mouthpiece" (communication channel) for the Kremlin and had ideologically aligned itself with Moscow.
  4. The Jean-Luc Schaffhauser affair: A former RN MEP who negotiated the Russian loan has come under investigation. Journalistic investigations showed that he received money from Russian contacts for delivering pro-Russian speeches in the European Parliament.

The party’s position and changes after 2022

Following the start of Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, the party's rhetoric changed noticeably:

  1. Condemnation of the invasion: Marine Le Pen stated that Vladimir Putin had "crossed a red line" and condemned the military actions.
  2. Distancing: Ahead of the 2024 elections, the party removed calls for deepening diplomatic ties with Russia and abandoning sanctions from its platforms. Le Pen is striving for "de- demonization" (shedding the radical image) and improving the party's image on the international arena in order to reassure voters and international partners.
  3. Official denial: Le Pen herself categorically denied during parliamentary hearings in 2023 that the loan or any other factors influenced the party's political positions. She emphasized that the deals were concluded with banks, not with Vladimir Putin, and that there was no "quid pro quo" (exchange of favors).

How the party explains its position

The National Rally rejects the binary division of the world into "pro-American" and "pro-Russian" forces. The party positions itself as "pro-French." Its ideology is based on:

  • Realism and multipolarity: Le Pen advocates for abandoning the hegemony of the US and NATO, proposing to build relations with Russia (as with other countries) on the basis of pragmatism rather than ideology.
  • National sovereignty: The party believes that France should conduct an independent foreign policy without looking back at Brussels or Washington, including on issues of energy cooperation and the security architecture in Europe.

Summary

Prior to 2022, the National Rally did indeed demonstrate open sympathy for Russia and Vladimir Putin, which was reinforced by financial deals with Russian structures and the voting of party deputies in the European Parliament against anti-Russian sanctions.

Following the start of the war in Ukraine, the party undertook efforts to distance itself from Moscow and soften its rhetoric. Nevertheless, its historical legacy, financial scandals, and ongoing legal proceedings prevent critics from removing the pro-Russian label from the party. At the same time, the nationalists themselves insist that their policy is exclusively pragmatic, realistic, and focused on France's sovereign interests.

Original text

Вопрос о том, является ли французская партия «Национальное объединение» (RN) Марин Ле Пен пророссийской, является предметом острых политических дискуссий во Франции и Европе.

С одной стороны, критики, политические оппоненты и ряд официальных отчетов указывают на глубокие исторические, финансовые и идеологические связи партии с Москвой. С другой стороны, сама партия и ее лидеры категорически отвергают ярлык «пророссийской», утверждая, что их внешняя политика продиктована исключительно национальными интересами Франции и принципами многополярности.

Ниже приведены основные факты и аргументы, которые формируют эту дискуссию.

Аргументы и факты, подтверждающие тесные связи с Россией

  1. Финансовая зависимость: В 2014 году, когда партия (тогда «Национальный фронт») испытывала серьезные финансовые трудности и французские банки отказывали ей в кредитах, она заняла 9,4 млн евро у Первого чешско-русского банка. Этот банк был связан с бизнесменом Геннадием Тимченко, входящим в близкое окружение Владимира Путина. Кредит был полностью погашен лишь в 2023–2024 годах. Вокруг его происхождения и влияния на партию во Франции до сих пор продолжаются судебные расследования.
  2. Политическое выравнивание (до 2022 года): После аннексии Крыма Россией в 2014 году Марин Ле Пен заявила, что результаты референдума «неоспоримы», что противоречило официальной позиции Парижа. Партия последовательно выступала против введения санкций ЕС в отношении России, а в 2017 году Ле Пен посетила Кремль, где встретилась с Путиным.
  3. Парламентский отчет: В 2023 году по итогам шестимесячного расследования французский парламентский комитет выпустил отчет, в котором заявил, что «Национальное объединение» долгое время выступало в роли «рупора» (канала связи) Кремля и идеологически сближалось с Москвой.
  4. Дело Жан-Люка Шаффхаузера: Бывший евродепутат от RN, который вел переговоры о российском кредите, попал под расследование. Журналистские расследования показали, что он получал деньги от российских контактов за произнесение пророссийских речей в Европарламенте.

Позиция партии и изменения после 2022 года

После начала полномасштабного вторжения России в Украину в феврале 2022 года риторика партии заметно изменилась:

  1. Осуждение вторжения: Марин Ле Пен заявила, что Владимир Путин «пересек красную линию», и осудила военные действия.
  2. Дистанцирование: В преддверии выборов 2024 года партия удалила из своих программ призывы к углублению дипломатических связей с Россией и отказу от санкций. Ле Пен стремится к «дедиaболиза́ции» (снятию образа радикалов) и улучшению имиджа партии на международной арене, чтобы успокоить избирателей и международных партнеров.
  3. Официальное отрицание: Сама Ле Пен на парламентских слушаниях в 2023 году категорически отрицала, что кредит или какие-либо иные факторы влияли на политические позиции партии. Она подчеркивала, что сделки заключались с банками, а не с Владимиром Путиным, и что никакого «обмена услугами» (quid pro quo) не было.

Как партия объясняет свою позицию

«Национальное объединение» отвергает бинарное деление мира на «проамериканские» и «пророссийские» силы. Партия позиционирует себя как «профранцузская». Ее идеология опирается на:

  • Реализм и многополярность: Ле Пен выступает за отказ от гегемонии США и НАТО, предлагая строить отношения с Россией (как и с другими странами) на основе прагматизма, а не идеологии.
  • Национальный суверенитет: Партия считает, что Франция должна вести самостоятельную внешнюю политику, не оглядываясь на Брюссель или Вашингтон, включая вопросы энергетического сотрудничества и архитектуры безопасности в Европе.

Резюме

До 2022 года «Национальное объединение» действительно демонстрировало открытую симпатию к России и Владимиру Путину, что подкреплялось финансовыми сделками с российскими структурами и голосованием депутатов партии в Европарламенте против антироссийских санкций.

После начала войны в Украине партия предприняла усилия, чтобы дистанцироваться от Москвы и смягчить свою риторику. Тем не менее, историческое наследие, финансовые скандалы и продолжающиеся судебные разбирательства не позволяют критикам снять с партии ярлык пророссийской. В то же время сами националисты настаивают на том, что их политика является исключительно прагматичной, реалистичной и ориентированной на суверенные интересы Франции.
